Otherwise `unknown` (453 greedy rows): unparseable candidates, `no_audited_gold` (the panel's correction failed to parse or verify) and `unknown_capped`. Unknown rows are kept and counted.\n\nMain metadata fields:\n- **Provenance**: `corpus`, `corpus_subset`, `source_id`, `story_id`, `licence`, `system`, `model`, `system_tier` (required/extra), `provider`, `sample_idx`, `temperature`, `raw_output`, `parse_ok`, `parse_error`, `parse_notes`, `finish_reason`, `prompt_sha1`, `extractor_version`, `gen_usd`, `gen_seconds`.\n- **Gold**:\n  - `gold_fol_original`, `gold_fol_audited`;\n  - `gold_source` ∈ {original, panel_corrected, panel_flagged_uncorrected (the panel flagged the gold but no correction parsed/verified, so `gold_fol_audited`=null and the label is unknown unless L3-adjudicated), paper_corrected}. In the greedy rows: original 3,087, panel_corrected 2,160, paper_corrected 891, panel_flagged_uncorrected 162;\n  - `gold_fol_paper` + `paper_corrected_flag` (the 2606.02837 human correction);\n  - `gold_fol_refined` (yfxiao/folio-refined, metadata only).\n- **L0**: `gold_audit_votes` (per member: faithful, ambiguous, error types, explanation, model), `gold_faithful_final`, `sentence_ambiguous`, `gold_audit_primary_error`, `gold_audit_cascade_rule`, `gold_audit_full_panel_subset`, `gold_correction_status`.\n- **L1**, for both `L1_orig_*` and `L1_audited_*`:\n  - `status` ∈ {equiv_proved, equiv_bounded, non_equiv, non_equiv_no_bijection, unknown_capped, unknown_timeout, unparseable, no_audited_gold};\n  - `mapping` (gold→candidate bijection);\n  - `entail_cand_to_gold` / `entail_gold_to_cand` (bounded one-directional hints under the best mapping);\n  - `method` (bounded/unbounded), `max_domain`, `seconds`.\n- **L2**: `L2_status` ∈ {equiv_granular, non_equiv, not_applicable, not_run_equivalent}, `L2_definitions`, `L2_lexical=true`, `L2_orig_status`.\n- **L3**:\n  - `L3_selected`;\n  - `L3_votes` (per member: cand_faithful, gold_faithful, error_types, primary_error, ambiguous, same_meaning, model);\n  - `L3_majority`, `L3_primary_error`, `L3_dissent`, `L3_gold_shown_as`;\n  - `L3_sampling_weight` (post-stratified N_h/n_h), `L3_poststratum`, `L3_design_weight`, `L3_design_stratum`;\n  - `L3_stratum_p_faithful` (estimated panel-faithful share of this row's post-stratum; a label-noise prior for non-adjudicated rows).\n- **Complexity**: `n_tokens`, `n_quantifiers`, `nesting_depth`, `n_conditions`, `complexity_composite`, `complexity_tercile`, `complexity_gold_used`.\n- **`label_source`**; **`source_verified`** / **`source_file`** (set by `data.py`).\n\n## 5. How labels were built\n\n**Panel.** Every member is family-disjoint from all 9 generators (Meta, Qwen, Mistral, OpenAI, Google, DeepSeek, Microsoft) and from the gemini judge baseline.\n\n| Member | Model | Calibration gate (40 known-label items, ≥80% required) | Note |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| M1 | anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5 | 0.90 | The planned claude-sonnet-4.5 **failed** at 0.775: it rejected valid contrapositive and material-implication rewrites. The plan's fallback was used |\n| M2 | x-ai/grok-4.3 (reasoning low) | 0.90 | grok-4-fast is no longer in the OpenRouter catalog |\n| M3 | z-ai/glm-4.6, **non-thinking** | 0.875 | Chosen for cost; the low-reasoning config scored 0.925 |\n\nThe calibration set:\n- 15 ProverQA-easy golds;\n- 15 solver-verified non-equivalent mutants: negation flip, ∀↔∃, implication reversal, dropped conjunct, and ∧↔∨ (used because ProverQA-easy has no binary predicates for argument swaps);\n- 10 solver-verified equivalent rewrites: contrapositive, material implication, predicate renaming.\n\nThe gate uses the original, pre-registered labels, even though 3 of the \"faithful\" golds are defensibly wrong.\n\n**L0 / L4 gold audit.** A cascade: M2 and M3 vote on every gold; M1 votes on disagreements plus a seeded 20% subset. There were 264 M1 tie-breaks among 1,060 golds.\n\nA correction is accepted only if it:\n- parses;\n- is satisfiable (bounded);\n- has a bounded countermodel against the original.\n\nOtherwise it is rejected: 33 corrections were equivalent to the original, and some were unparseable because they used function terms. For the 99 MALLS items with a human correction, `gold_fol_audited` = the paper's gold.\n\n**L1** is lexical-free.\n- It checks the arity profile first. A mismatch gives `non_equiv_no_bijection`.\n- It then tries all bijections, capped at 5,040 (only 2 greedy items hit the cap).\n- Only countermodels count as proofs of non-equivalence.\n- `equiv_proved` needs an unbounded z3 `unsat`. All equivalences in this run were proved, so no `equiv_bounded` items occur.\n\nValidation on the 59 parseable pairs among the paper's first 60 MALLS items: every uncorrected pair was equivalent (37/37) and every corrected pair was non-equivalent (22/22). Mean cost is about 6 ms per pair.\n\n**L3** is blinded and symmetric: gold and candidate appear as Formula A / Formula B in seeded random order, with no source or system named.\n- The design sample was 460: 400 non-equivalent, with the top tercile oversampled 1.5×, including 40 of the 77 L2-granular items; plus 60 equivalent.\n- 150 extra items were drawn uniformly at random within the top-tercile non-equivalent strata.\n- Estimates use post-stratified weights on the final frame. The frame has 5,848 greedy parseable candidates with audited gold; every stratum has at least 3 adjudicated items.\n\n**E1.**\n- 203 sentences carried a named entity or constant: FOLIO 187, MALLS only 16, because MALLS gold is almost constant-free. So the planned 75/75 split became 187/16.\n- gpt-4.1-mini paraphrased each sentence and renamed its entities. The renaming was applied to the gold constants and predicate substrings.\n- M2 verified both the meaning and the renamed gold. 104 of 203 passed (target was ≥120). Most drops are \"the renamed gold is not faithful to the paraphrase\", which reflects residual gold problems.\n- 124 of 936 renamed candidates are flagged `contamination_rename_incomplete`.\n\n## 6. Deviations from the plan (honest list)\n\n1. **Corrected gold WAS found.** arXiv:2606.02837's release is on HF as `DSAVlab-UNIUD/MALLS_test_subset-CURATED` (100 items = MALLS-test indices 0–99, verified by NL match) and `DSAVlab-UNIUD/FOLIO_validation-curated` (275 FOLIO-v2-validation instances: 73 whole-story premise blocks plus 202 conclusions). The paper's GitHub repo is still 404.\n   - The FOLIO file corrects stories and v2 conclusions, not v1 premises. So the L4 panel-vs-human check covers only the 22 screen conclusions whose v1 gold equals the v2 gold the paper judged (κ = 0.18, n = 22: too small to conclude anything).\n2. **Panel substitutions**: haiku-4.5 for sonnet-4.5 (failed the gate), grok-4.3 for the unavailable grok-4-fast, glm-4.6 non-thinking. See §5.\n3. **ProverQA was audited in full** (the 10% escalation rule fired). Its flagged golds use panel-corrected gold (`gold_source=panel_corrected`).\n4. **L3 was enlarged** from 300 to 610 adjudicated items (460 design + 150 extra top-tercile), funded from the reserve. Weights are post-stratified.\n5. **A parser bug was found and fixed after L0.** v1 read constants such as `yr2028`/`p1080` as free variables and universally closed them. It affected 8 golds (4 held-out) and 9 candidates.\n   - The drawn sample was kept frozen.\n   - The 4 affected sentences' complexity features were recomputed with the stored z-parameters (the old values are in `complexity_v1_buggy`).\n   - All L1/L2 labels were recomputed from scratch; the old cache is kept as `work/l1_cache_v1_buggy.jsonl`.\n   - The panel corrections were re-derived (0 changed).\n   - E1 verification was re-keyed on content. This re-called all 203 verifications, costing $0.49 of waste.\n   - 3 L3 items changed stratum; post-stratification absorbs this.\n6. **Extractor v2**: phi-4 answered in LaTeX (`\\(\\forall x \\, \\text{P}(x)\\)`). A formatting fix, not a prompt change, raised its parse rate from 68% to 88%. All rows were re-extracted from the stored `raw_output`.\n7. **The ASCII `^` is read as ∧, not ⊕**, because LLM outputs use it as a look-alike of ∧.\n8. The **folio-refined** signal barely agrees with the panel: κ = 0.004 on 416 FOLIO items; refined differs from the original on 34%, the panel flags 51%. Its (undocumented) refinement targets something other than faithfulness, e.g. cross-story vocabulary alignment. It is kept as metadata only.\n9. **Class balance**: 62% of labelled greedy rows are unfaithful, above the 25–60% target. This is inflated by the L1 label noise described in §1. Per corpus: ProverQA 42%, MALLS 66%, FOLIO 75%.\n10. **\"Heavily conditioned\" means connective-heavy, not exception-heavy.** The public sentence-level gold corpora contain almost no `unless`/`except` sentences, so the top tercile is driven by multiple connectives, ⊕, ↔ and quantifier nesting. E2 (EU AI Act) is the only exception-rich material, and it is unlabeled.\n11. Temperature-0 output is not bit-deterministic across OpenRouter providers. The provider is recorded per row, and `raw_output` is the ground truth of what was scored.\n12. `kenken6696/folio_by_ccg2lambda` and `kenken6696/MALLS_by_ccg2lambda` were downloaded (to `temp/datasets/`) as possible iter-2 non-LLM systems but **not used**. Their neo-Davidsonian event FOL has no predicate bijection to gold, so they could only be adjudicated by the panel.\n\n## 7. Dataset search and selection (TODOs 2–5)\n\n**Search.** 40 broad HF queries (`temp/hf_search.txt`), including: folio, MALLS, ProverQA, first-order logic, FOL, autoformalization, logical reasoning, semantic parsing, ProofWriter, LogicNLI, theorem proving, lambda calculus, monotonicity, logic-lm, …\n\n**Previews.** 20 candidates were previewed through the HF datasets-server API (`temp/previews/candidate_previews.json`; the skill's own preview script lacked the `datasets` module). 14 provenance web searches are in `temp/websearch/`.\n\n**KEPT (10):**\n\n| Dataset | Downloads | Licence | Role |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| tasksource/folio | 6.2k | FOLIO MIT | FOLIO-v2 train/validation (yale-nlp/FOLIO is gated for this token) |\n| yuan-yang/MALLS-v0 | 158 | CC BY-NC 4.0 | MALLS-v0.1-test (Yang et al. 2023) |\n| DSAVlab-UNIUD/MALLS_test_subset-CURATED | 26 | — | Human corrections from arXiv:2606.02837. Low downloads, but provenance is verified: card, paper and authors |\n| DSAVlab-UNIUD/FOLIO_validation-curated | 61 | — | Same paper; FOLIO side |\n| opendatalab/ProverQA | 380 | — | ICLR 2025, dev easy/medium/hard via `hf_hub_download`; `load_dataset` breaks |\n| yfxiao/folio-refined | 20 | MIT | Independent wrong-gold signal (method undocumented; metadata only) |\n| Yale-LILY/FOLIO GitHub v0.0 | — | — | v1 validation/train (screen gold) |\n| teacherpeterpan/Logic-LLM `outputs/logic_programs/FOLIO_dev_*.json` | — | — | Screen candidates |\n| User `dpv_pilot_study.zip` | — | — | E2 |\n| kenken6696/{folio,MALLS}_by_ccg2lambda | — | CC BY 4.0 | Downloaded for iter 2, unused |\n\n**DISCARDED**, each with its reason:\n\n| Dataset | Reason |\n|---|---|\n| benlipkin/folio, minimario/FOLIO, jhkim64/NL2FOL_sentence | Duplicate FOLIO mirrors, or <10 downloads with no card |\n| tasksource/FOL-nli (182 MB), tasksource/LogicNLI, tasksource/proofwriter, logicreasoning/logi_glue (963 MB), kkkarry/LogicGraph (698 MB) | NLI/QA with TPTP or proof annotations; no sentence-level NL→FOL gold, and some are too large |\n| Isotonic/symbolic_data_first_order_logic | Arithmetic \"FOL\"; no card |\n| verify-ppt/ppt-logic_first_order | No loadable data |\n| tasksource/monotonicity-entailment (MED) | Used by screen Arm A, not a FOL corpus |\n\n## 8. How to run (in order)\n\n```bash\nuv venv .venv --python=3.12 && uv pip install --python=.venv/bin/python z3-solver nltk aiohttp loguru huggingface_hub numpy scipy tenacity requests pyyaml pytest\nbash restore.sh                                    # sources + nltk data + pilot extraction\n.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -c pytest.ini tests/test_fol.py\n.venv/bin/python src/prep_sources.py               # steps 1-3 (no API)\n.venv/bin/python src/generate.py --concurrency 24  # step 4 (~$0.72; resumable)\n.venv/bin/python src/reextract.py                  # extractor v2\n.venv/bin/python src/label_l1.py --stage orig\n.venv/bin/python src/calibrate.py && .venv/bin/python src/calibrate_variants.py   # (~$0.88)\n.venv/bin/python src/audit.py --cap 4.0            # L0 + L4 (~$3.43)\n.venv/bin/python src/fix_varlike_patch.py          # only needed for artefacts produced by parser v1\n.venv/bin/python src/label_l1.py --stage audited\n.venv/bin/python src/l3_adjudicate.py --n-non 400 --n-eq 60 --cap 2.8   # (~$2.46)\n.venv/bin/python src/l3_extra.py --n 150 --cap 0.85                      # (~$0.88)\n.venv/bin/python src/e1_contamination.py --n 203 --cap 0.75              # (~$0.86)\n.venv/bin/python src/e2_transfer.py\n.venv/bin/python src/assemble.py                  # -> work/assembled_data_out.json.gz + label_report.json\nuv run data.py                                     # source verification -> full_data_out.json (validated: exp_sel_data_out)\n.venv/bin/python src/split_outputs.py             # -> full_data_out/full_data_out_{1,2,3}.json + mini/preview\n```\n\nEvery panel and generation response is cached (`work/panel_cache.jsonl`, `raw/generations/`), so a re-run of the labelling steps costs $0.\n\nTotal spend: **$9.22**:\n\n| Phase | Cost |\n|---|---|\n| Generation | $0.72 |\n| Calibration | $0.88 |\n| Gold audit | $3.43 |\n| L3 | $2.46 + $0.88 |\n| E1 | $0.86 |\n\nIt stayed under the $9.30 hard cap, with the ledger updated after every call.\n\n## 9. Restoring removed files\n\nThese paths are deleted after the round (see `.aii/manifest.yaml`). `bash restore.sh` recreates all of them. It also re-downloads `raw/hf_downloads/` and the ccg2lambda files in `temp/datasets/` if they are missing; those are small and are kept.\n\n| Path | Restore with |\n|---|---|\n| `raw/pilot/` | `python -c \"import zipfile; zipfile.ZipFile('/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_qY2a2IS-WLIs/user_uploads/dpv_pilot_study.zip').extractall('raw/pilot')\"` |\n| `nltk_data/` | `python -c \"import nltk; [nltk.download(p, download_dir='nltk_data') for p in ('wordnet','omw-1.4')]\"` |\n| `.venv/` | The `uv venv …` line in §8 |\n| `__pycache__/`, `.pytest_cache/` | Regenerated automatically |\n", "numLines": 291, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 291}}

function terms)?\"\"\"\n    ast, err = fc.try_parse(fol)\n    return {\"score\": float(ast is not None), \"error_class\": err}\n\n\ndef tvjt_score(text: str, fol: str, seed_key: str = \"api\") -> \"covered\": False, \"reason\": f\"unparseable:{err}\"}\n    worlds = worlds_for(F, seed_key)\n    if not worlds:\n        return {\"score\": 0.5, \"covered\": False, \"reason\": \"no_world\"}\n    prompt = build_prompt(text, F, worlds)\n\n    async def run():\n        async with LLM(concurrency=1) as L:\n            return await L.call([{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": prompt}], stage=\"api_TVJT\", item_id=seed_key)\n    r = asyncio.run(run())\n    ans = parse_answer(r[\"text\"], len(worlds))\n    if ans is None:\n        return {\"score\": 0.5, \"covered\": False, \"reason\": \"malformed\"}\n    sc, agrees, et = score_answers(worlds, ans)\n    return {\"score\": sc, \"covered\": True, \"error_type\": et, \"agrees\": agrees, \"ops\": [w[\"op\"] for w in worlds],\n            \"usd\": r[\"cost\"]}\n\n\n_NLI = {}\n\n\ndef instance_nli_score(text: str, fol: str, seed_key: str = \"api\") -> dict:\n    from instance_nli import agree_scores, error_type, process_target\n    from stages import deberta_probs\n    F, err = fc.try_parse(fol)\n    if F is None:\n        return {\"score\": 0.5, \"covered\": False, \"reason\": f\"unparseable:{err}\"}\n    c = process_target(F, text, seed_key)\n    if len(c[\"labels\"]) < 3:\n        return {\"score\": 0.5, \"covered\": False, \"reason\": \"few_hypotheses\"}\n    pr = deberta_probs(c[\"texts\"])\n    sb, s3 = agree_scores(c[\"labels\"], pr)\n    return {\"score\": sb, \"score_3way\": s3, \"covered\": True,\n            \"error_type\": error_type(c[\"labels\"], c[\"pairs\"], pr, c[\"mutant_labels\"]),\n            \"pairs\": list(zip(c[\"texts\"], c[\"labels\"]))}\n\n\ndef latent_class_scores(fols_by_system: dict[str, str]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Single-sentence use: agreement share (MAJ). For the EM fit over a corpus see latent_class.run_latent_class.\"\"\"\n    asts = {s: fc.try_parse(f)[0] for s, f in fols_by_system.items()}\n    ok = [s for s, a in asts.items() if a is not None]\n    out = {}\n    for s in fols_by_system:\n        if asts[s] is None or len(ok) < 2:\n            out[s] = {\"score\": 0.5 if asts[s] is not None else 0.0, \"covered\": False}\n            continue\n        agree = sum(1 for t in ok if t != s and fc.find_bijections(asts[s], asts[t])[\"label\"] == \"equiv\")\n        out[s] = {\"score\": agree / (len(ok) - 1), \"covered\": True}\n    return out\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    print(parse_ok(\"∀x (Dog(x) → Bark(x))\"))\n    print(latent_class_scores({\"a\": \"∀x (Dog(x) → Bark(x))\", \"b\": \"∀x (Canine(x) → Barks(x))\", \"c\": \"∃x (Dog(x) ∧ Bark(x))\"}))\n", "numLines": 103, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 103}}

The work addresses the lack of reliable evaluation metrics for comparing generated and ground-truth FOLs.\n\n## Methodology\n\nThe study evaluates the sensitivity of existing NL-, FOL-, and graph-based metrics to capture differences between sampled FOLs and ground-truth, measures alignment between metric-based ranking of FOL outputs and a strong LLM as-a-judge, and applies operator and text-based perturbations to ground-truth FOL statements to assess metric sensitivity.\n\n## Key Findings\n\nThe empirical findings highlight oversensitivity in the n-gram metric BLEU for text perturbations. Operator perturbation affects the semantic graph metric Smatch++ for structural changes, and the FOL metric for specific operator changes. There is closer alignment between BertScore and LLM judgement, proving the importance of semantic evaluation. Additionally, combining metrics enhances both robustness and sensitivity compared to using individual metrics."], "durationSeconds": 7.357573703999995, "searchCount": 1}

Match exactly how iter-1 llm.py sent it, and check it in the copied code.\n- Sanity: re-send 5 iter-1 TVJT prompts through the cache; they must hit the cache at $0.\n\nStage budgets (soft stops):\n- P1 repair on the screen: $1.6\n- P1b test-retest: $0.15\n- P2a panel items, all LLM metrics: $2.6\n- P2c contamination: $0.9\n- P2b top-tercile expansion: $3.0\n- flash-lite variant: $0.4\n- held-out G2 replication: $0.2\n- reserve: $0.65\nOrder of execution = P1 → freeze → P2a → P2c → P2b → the rest, so the most valuable held-out evidence is bought first.\n\n=== 1. SYNTAX BRIDGE (held-out candidates are not all FOLIO-unicode) ===\ndef to_folio(fol_str):\n    ast, err = fc.try_parse(fol_str)          # Arm B parser first\n    if ast: return ast, 'direct'\n    a2 = ds_fol_parse.parse(fol_str)           # dataset tolerant parser (ASCII ^ read as AND, LaTeX, snake_case)\n    s2 = ds_fol_parse.to_unicode_string(a2)    # use its printer; check the function name in fol_parse.py\n    ast, err = fc.try_parse(s2)\n    return (ast, 'bridged') if ast else (None, 'unparseable:' + err)\nLog the bridge coverage per system and corpus. Coverage loss is COUNTED: an uncovered item gets score 0.5 and stays in every AUROC (the iter-1 convention). Every AUROC is also reported on the covered-by-all-metrics subset.\n\n=== 2. R1: CANONICAL FORM + canonical mutants/worlds (src/canon.py) ===\ndef canon(F):\n  1. Eliminate ↔ and ⊕ only when they sit under a negation; otherwise keep them, to limit blow-up.\n  2. NNF: push ¬ to atoms. ¬∀ becomes ∃¬ and ¬∃ becomes ∀¬; De Morgan is applied; ¬(A→B) becomes A∧¬B.\n  3. Restricted-implication normalisation: every implication A→B, and every disjunction directly under ∀ that contains negative literals, is rewritten as (conjunction of the POSITIVE forms of the negative disjuncts) → (disjunction of the remaining disjuncts).\n     - So A→B, ¬B→¬A and ¬A∨B all map to one form. This kills the CONTRAPOSITIVE and DEMORGAN false alarms.\n     - If every disjunct is negative, the consequent is the ¬literal that comes last in skeleton order.\n  4. Prenex with mutants.prenex. Keep the original if prenexing fails (NotPrenexable), and set the flag.\n  5. Flatten ∧ and ∨ and sort their operands by the NAME-BLIND skeleton string: predicates replaced by 'P/arity', constants by 'c', variables alpha-renamed. Ties are broken by the full string.\n  6. Alpha-rename bound variables to x, y, z, w, … in order of first occurrence.\n  7. Size guard: if the canonical tree has more than 3× the original node count, use the original with canon_fallback=True.\n  8. Verify the canonical form: bounded_equiv(canon(F), F, nmax=4) must be 'equiv'. Otherwise fall back to the original and set the flag.\n  canon_seed = sha1(skeleton string of canon(F)). This is name-blind, so a RENAME rewrite gets the same seed.\n\ndef canonical_worlds(F):\n  Fc = canon(F)\n  For op in OPERATORS (CARD with variants 'two' and 'unique', as iter-1 card_variants=True):\n      m = make_mutant(Fc, op, canon_seed)          # canonical seed, never item_id\n  Deduplicate the kept mutants by pairwise bounded_equiv; keep the first in OPERATORS order.\n  For each distinct mutant: w = find_world(Fc, m, prefer=0 fixed, nmax=4), with z3.set_param('smt.random_seed', 0) and 'sat.random_seed' 0.\n  Deduplicate worlds by isomorphism: canonical key = sorted true_atoms after relabeling elements in order of first appearance. Take the lexicographic minimum over the ≤4! element permutations.\n  Cap at 12 worlds. Record per world: op, F_value, world_key, and n.\n  Order the worlds canonically by world_key.\nCACHE: data/worlds_canon.jsonl, keyed by sha1(to_str(Fc)). Identical canonical forms across systems and rewrites reuse the worlds.\n\nZERO-COST DIAGNOSTIC, run BEFORE any LLM call: for each of the 499 screen rewrites, check whether it has the same world multiset as its source gold, up to predicate renaming for RENAME.\n- Report the 'world-set identity rate' per rewrite kind.\n- Expected: REORDER, DEMORGAN and CONTRAPOSITIVE at 90% or more. If a kind is below 70%, debug canon() before spending any money.\n\n=== 3. R2+R3: JUDGE PROTOCOL (src/judge.py) ===\nPrompt 'tvjt_v2_conf' is iter-1 TVJT_PROMPT with the SAME HEADER and verbaliser. The answer instruction becomes:\n  'Answer with JSON only, one entry per situation: {\"1\": [v, c], ...} where v is 1 if the sentence is TRUE in that situation and 0 if FALSE, and c is your confidence 0-100.'\nThis compact format keeps output tokens low.\n\nWorld-level cache:\n- key_w = sha1(model | prompt_version | sentence | world_text | vote_idx).\n- For a target, gather its worlds W in canonical order and drop those already cached for vote k.\n- Send the uncached ones as ONE batched call, with situations presented in order perm_k(U).\n  - perm_0 = canonical order, perm_1 = reversed, perm_2 = rotated by ceil(|U|/2).\n  - The situation labels 1..|U| follow the presented order. Answers are mapped back to worlds and stored per (world, k).\n- A malformed answer gets one retry with a 'JSON only' reminder. If it still fails, those worlds are uncovered for that vote.\n\nScore definitions per target:\n  p_k(w) = c/100 if v==1 else 1 − c/100          # judged P(sentence TRUE in w)\n  agree_bin_k(w) = 1[v_k==F_value(w)]\n  C1 = mean_w agree_bin_0(w)                      # canonical worlds, 1 vote\n  C2 = mean_w 1[majority_k v_k == F_value(w)]     # 3-vote majority\n  C3 = mean_w (p̄(w) if F_value(w) else 1 − p̄(w)),  p̄ = mean_k p_k(w)   # continuous, confidence-weighted (PRE-SELECTED default)\n  error_type = the operator family with the lowest mean agreement (ties broken by OPERATORS order), or 'none' if all agreements are ≥ 0.5.\nOne call serves C1, C2 and C3, so the grid costs 3 calls per uncached world-set and no more.\n\n=== 4. PHASE 1: SCREEN REPAIR (labels allowed = screen only) ===\nTargets:\n- 833 real screen candidates;\n- the 300 golds;\n- the 499 rewrites.\nWith the world cache, expect about 1.6k targets and about 900 distinct world-sets.\n\nConfigurations:\n- C0 = iter-1 TVJT, from the copied screen_scores and cache ($0).\n- C0' = the iter-1 prompt on canonical worlds, vote 0. Run only on the 300 golds and 499 rewrites (≈$0.15). This isolates the R1 effect from the prompt change.\n- C1, C2, C3 as defined in section 3.\nScale-up: 50 targets → 300 → all. At each step, check cost and time per target and extrapolate against the stage budget.\n\nMetrics per configuration:\n- AUROC_real under L_bij, which is the iter-1 label and gives a like-for-like comparison with .701. Use a 2000× sentence-clustered bootstrap.\n- Secondary: AUROC under the screen_gold_audit panel-audited label, joined on sentence_id and system.\n- Per tercile.\n- Paired G2 FA: the share of rewrites r with score(r) < score(gold_of_r) − 0.2. Report it overall, for non-RENAME kinds, and per kind.\n- The iter-1 absolute G2 at τ* (τ* chosen by Youden on the screen) and at 0.5.\n- Per-operator pairwise detection on gold worlds.\n- Test-retest (P1b): 150 random screen targets are re-called in a separate cache namespace 'retest'.\n  - Report per-world verdict agreement with Cohen κ.\n  - Report the score ICC(2,1) between the two runs for C1 and for C3.\n  - Report how many worlds have 3-vote disagreement.\n\nFREEZE RULE (pre-declared here; do not change after seeing results):\n- A configuration is ELIGIBLE if (a) its paired G2 FA over ALL rewrites is ≤ 0.10, and (b) its screen AUROC_real(L_bij) is ≥ 0.701 − 0.02 = 0.681.\n- Pick the eligible configuration with the highest AUROC_real. If two are within 0.01, pick the one with fewer calls: C1 < C2 = C3, then prefer C3 for threshold-freeness.\n- If none is eligible only because of RENAME (non-RENAME FA ≤ 0.10), pick by the same AUROC rule and record 'G2_all_failed_by_RENAME'. The RENAME FA is then reported as the residual verbaliser/synonym limitation.\n- If none is eligible at all, go to FALLBACK F1/F2, still on the screen only. Freeze the best configuration anyway, with frozen_G2_pass=false. The crossover question is tested regardless.\n- Choose the binary threshold τ_frozen by Youden on the screen real items. It is used only for the binary G2 and detection rates.\n\nWrite results/frozen_config.json:\n- config id, prompt text and version, model, T=0, the vote scheme, the scoring formula, τ_frozen and the canon() options;\n- git hash or sha1 of src/*.py;\n- the screen metrics that justified it.\nWrite results/prereg.json with every PHASE-2 hypothesis, test, bin edge and decision rule listed in section 7. Hash both files into results/freeze_receipt.json, with a UTC timestamp. Only then may the held-out LABEL loader run: src/labels.py asserts that freeze_receipt.json exists and that its hashes match.\nThe held-out SCORING code reads ONLY the input, metadata_item_id, metadata_sentence_id, metadata_system, metadata_corpus and complexity fields. It never reads output or metadata_L3_* fields.\n\n=== 5. PHASE 2: HELD-OUT SCORING (label-blind) ===\nLoad full_data_out_*.json. Sort the parts numerically, following the dataset README loader.\n\nItem sets:\n- P = heldout_confirm rows with metadata_label_source=='panel3' (609).\n- T = heldout_confirm rows in the top tercile. Check the encoding of metadata_complexity_tercile (0/1/2 or labels) from label_report.json; the top tercile is the highest-composite one. Expect about 316 sentences × 9 = 2,844 rows, all systems, including unparseable ones.\n- Ccon = contamination rows for systems {gpt-4.1-mini, deepseek-v3.1, llama-3.1-8b}, each paired with its original heldout_confirm row via metadata_original_item_id. That gives 312 pairs, or 624 targets. Extend to all 9 systems if budget remains after P2b.\n- S_all = all 6,300 heldout_confirm rows. These are needed ONLY for the zero-LLM LC fit and for B3sc.\n\nMetrics on P ∪ T ∪ Ccon, identical items throughout:\n- TVJT* (frozen), with all 3 votes.\n- TVJT_iter1: the unrepaired iter-1 code (seed=item_id, iter-1 prompt, 1 vote), on P and T only. This is the within-artifact ablation.\n- B1: the EXACT iter-1 fixed prompt from results/b1_request_example.json and prompts_frozen.json, on gemini-2.5-flash at T=0 with reasoning off, 1 call, output a 0-100 probability.\n- B1x3: the same prompt with 3 calls. Vote_idx 1 and 2 are sent with the identical prompt and a cache bypass; take the mean of the 3. Run it on P and on T if budget allows. This is the COMPUTE-MATCHED judge.\n- TVJT_lite: the frozen configuration with google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite as judge, on P only.\n- parse_ok, both as metadata_parse_ok and as fol_core-bridge coverage.\n- LC_onecoin (zero-LLM):\n  - For every sentence in S_all, compute pairwise fc.find_bijections(a_i, a_j)['label'] among its 9 parseable candidates. That is up to 36 pairs × 700 sentences, run on a ProcessPool with 4 workers, spawn, wall 10 s per pair; a timeout counts as not-equivalent and is logged.\n  - Then run latent_class.run_latent_class (the one-coin EM with an all-wrong state) over all 700 sentences, giving a posterior per candidate.\n- A3: third_party/armA method.signature_faithfulness(sentence, bridged_fol_str, variant='A3'), on P ∪ T.\n- B3sc (zero-LLM, standard self-consistency baseline):\n  - For gpt-4.1-mini and llama-3.1-8b greedy rows, take the share of that system's 5 heldout_samples for the same sentence that are find_bijections-equivalent to the greedy candidate.\n  - Available on 2/9 systems only; report it on that subset.\nPersist everything to results/heldout_scores.jsonl, one row per (item_id, metric), with score, covered, error_type_pred, n_worlds, usd, seconds, canon_fallback and bridge.\n\nOPTIONAL, if budget remains (P2d): held-out G2 replication, which needs no labels.\n- Take 100 held-out audited golds (metadata_gold_fol_audited not null, sentence-stratified by corpus).\n- For each, run mutants.make_rewrites(k=2), which gives about 200 rewrites. Score the golds and the rewrites with TVJT*.\n- Report the paired FA per kind. Expected cost is about $0.2.\n\n=== 6. LABELS (src/labels.py; runs only after freeze_receipt) ===\n- y_panel (P): metadata_L3_majority mapped to 1 = faithful, with weight w = metadata_L3_sampling_weight.\n- y_hard (T and other rows): output in {faithful, unfaithful}. unknown is excluded from y_hard metrics and COUNTED.\n- y_soft:\n  - panel rows: y_panel;\n  - otherwise p_faithful = metadata_L3_stratum_p_faithful when present;\n  - else 0.90 for L1-equivalent rows and 0.44 for L1-non-equivalent rows, from label_report.json (9.9% of equivalent rows are unfaithful; 43.9% of non-equivalent rows are faithful).\n- Contamination: the output label transferred to the renamed row. Rows with metadata_contamination_rename_incomplete are flagged and reported with and without them.\n\n=== 7. ANALYSES (src/analysis2.py; every number goes to results/analysis.json) ===\nA. Weighted AUROC on P for every metric:\n   AUC_w = Σ_{i∈pos, j∈neg} w_i w_j [1(s_i>s_j) + .5·1(s_i=s_j)] / (Σw_pos · Σw_neg)\n   - CI: 2000× cluster bootstrap that resamples sentence_ids within corpus strata (seed 0), reusing the SAME resamples for all metrics so the deltas are paired.\n   - Also report the unweighted AUROC and the within-sentence pairwise accuracy (a faithful candidate outscores an unfaithful candidate of the same sentence, ties 0.5), over sentences with mixed panel or L1-equivalent labels.\n   - Also report every AUROC excluding gemini-2.5-flash-generated candidates, because the judge model is also a generator.\nB. PRE-REGISTERED CROSSOVER (the primary question). Bins: complexity tercile (bottom/mid/top) and n_conditions bins {0-1, 2-3, 4+}.\n   - H1 (primary): on P restricted to the top tercile, ΔAUROC_w(TVJT* − B1) > 0, with the paired-bootstrap 95% CI lower bound > 0.\n     Power switch, decided BEFORE labels are read, from label-free counts: if the top-tercile panel count is < 150 or has fewer than 40 of either class-agnostic... (use the count only), H1 is evaluated on T with y_soft (soft AUROC, below), and P-top becomes secondary.\n   - H2 (growth): Δ_top − Δ_bottom > 0 (paired bootstrap CI), and the Δ per n_conditions bin is monotone non-decreasing (reported with CIs).\n   - H2b (logistic interaction):\n     - Per metric m, fit a weighted GLM (statsmodels, Binomial, freq_weights = w, cluster-robust SE by sentence_id): y ~ z(s_m) + c + z(s_m):c, with c = complexity_composite (and, separately, n_conditions).\n     - Report the interaction slope β3 with its CI.\n     - Joint model: y ~ z(s_TVJT)·c + z(s_B1)·c. Test β(TVJT:c) − β(B1:c) > 0 with a bootstrap CI.\n   - Soft-label AUROC on T: each item contributes as a positive with weight p and as a negative with weight 1−p (probabilistic ROC).\n     - Also run multiple imputation: 200 Bernoulli(p) label draws, AUROC per draw, report the mean and the 2.5–97.5% interval, pooled with the cluster bootstrap.\n     - Also report the hard-label (y_hard) AUROC on T, flagged as biased toward gold vocabulary.\n   - Repeat B for LC_onecoin, A3, B1x3, TVJT_iter1 and TVJT_lite, to locate which component carries the long-sentence regime.\n     Key contrasts: TVJT* − B1x3 (compute-matched) and TVJT* − TVJT_iter1 (did the repair cost signal?).\nC. REAL ERROR TYPES (P, unfaithful items grouped by metadata_L3_primary_error).\n   - Detection for type e = P(score of the erroneous item < score of a faithful candidate for the SAME sentence). Faithful comparators are panel-faithful or L1-equivalent candidates. Ties count 0.5.\n   - Fallback when a sentence has no faithful comparator: compare against all panel-faithful items (the per-type AUROC).\n   - Types: quantifier_scope, cardinality, quantifier_forall_exists, added_condition, dropped_condition, and/or (use the dataset's exact strings), plus implication_direction_or_only.\n   - Report n, detection and Wilson or bootstrap CIs for TVJT*, B1, A3 and LC.\n   - Head-to-head on {quantifier_scope ∪ cardinality}: TVJT* − A3, where A3 is predicted near 0.5.\n   - Supplement with the screen blindspot_supplement: SCOPE_SWAP n=37 and CARD, pairwise detection on gold worlds under the frozen protocol.\n   - Error-type IDENTIFICATION: map TVJT error_type operator families to L3 types (NEG→negation, QUANT→forall_exists, SCOPE_SWAP→scope, CARD→cardinality, DROP_CONJ→dropped, ADD_CONJ→added, IMPL_REV→implication, AND_OR→and_or, ARG_SWAP→swapped_args, MERGE→conflation). Report the confusion matrix and macro-F1; this is secondary.\nD. STACKING on P (and on T with y_hard as secondary):\n   - Cross-fitted logistic regression (GroupKFold by sentence_id, 5 folds, sample weights w, standardized features, C=1.0, fixed).\n   - Feature sets:\n     - S0 = [B1, parse_ok]\n     - S1 = S0 + TVJT*\n     - S2 = S1 + LC + A3\n     - S0z = S0 + LC + A3 (the zero-LLM stack without TVJT)\n   - Report the OOF AUROC_w and the paired-bootstrap ΔAUROC for S1−S0, S2−S0z (TVJT's marginal value over the full gold-free stack) and S2−S0.\n   - Placebo: S0 + a shuffled TVJT column, whose Δ CI must contain 0.\nE. CONTAMINATION (Ccon): per metric, AUROC on the originals vs the paraphrases (paired bootstrap over the 104 pairs), the mean score shift orig→para per label class, and |Δ|.\n   The prediction is TVJT Δ≈0 and B1 possibly negative. Report with and without rename_incomplete rows.\nF. SYSTEM LEVEL: Kendall τ between each system's mean metric score and its accuracy.\n   - Accuracy on P is w-weighted; the y_soft accuracy uses all heldout_confirm rows.\n   - Bootstrap CI over sentences. Note that with 9 systems |τ| ≥ 0.61 is needed for p < .05.\nG. COST CURVE: $/item (fresh-call cost amortised over cache reuse, and also the no-cache cost) and median seconds per item.\n   Plot AUROC_w on P against $/item for B1, B1x3, TVJT_lite, TVJT* with 1 vote (C1-equivalent from vote 0) and TVJT* with 3 votes.\nH. COVERAGE: bridge failures, no-world rate, malformed-JSON rate and canon_fallback rate, per system and corpus. Verbaliser failure rate counted as in iter 1.\nI. Figures (aii-data-fig-gen):\n   (1) the AUROC by tercile / n_conditions bin line plot for TVJT*, B1, B1x3, LC and A3, with CIs;\n   (2) the ΔAUROC(TVJT*−B1) forest plot by bin;\n   (3) the paired-FA per rewrite kind, iter-1 vs frozen;\n   (4) the cost-accuracy scatter;\n   (5) the real-error-type detection bars.\n\nDECISION TABLE (written into prereg.json and applied verbatim in results/verdict.json):\n- CROSSOVER_CONFIRMED: H1 lower bound > 0 AND H2 Δ_top − Δ_bottom lower bound > 0.\n- CROSSOVER_PARTIAL: H1 holds, H2 is not significant.\n- NOT_REPLICATED: the H1 CI includes 0.\n- REVERSED: the H1 upper bound < 0.\n- Separately:\n  - REPAIR_OK if the frozen screen G2 passed and the held-out G2 replication (if run) has paired FA ≤ 0.10;\n  - JUDGE_MATCHED if TVJT* − B1x3 in the top tercile has a lower bound > 0 (otherwise the advantage may be vote averaging);\n  - STACK_INCREMENT if the S2 − S0z lower bound > 0.\n\n=== 8. OUTPUTS ===\n- method.py: the orchestrator, run as --stage setup|canon_diag|repair|freeze|score_heldout|labels|analysis|export [--limit N].\n- src/metrics_api2.py exposes the reusable functions:\n  - tvjt_score(text, fol, config='frozen') -> {score, covered, error_type, worlds, agrees, usd}\n  - canon(fol_str) -> str\n  - distinguishing_worlds(fol_str) -> list\n  Docstrings state precisely what each one measures.\n- method_out.json in the exp_gen_sol_out schema, validated with aii-json. It holds a dataset per group: screen_real, screen_rewrites, heldout_panel, heldout_top, contamination. Each example has input, output (label), predict_TVJT_frozen, predict_TVJT_iter1, predict_B1, predict_B1x3, predict_TVJT_lite, predict_LC, predict_A3 and predict_B3sc (as strings), plus metadata_* fields.\n  If it exceeds the limit, split it with aii-file-size-limit, and create the mini and preview variants.\n- results/: frozen_config.json, prereg.json, freeze_receipt.json, analysis.json, verdict.json, heldout_scores.jsonl, screen_repair_scores.jsonl, cost_ledger.jsonl.\n- README.md and .aii/manifest.yaml:\n  - keep: cache/llm*, data/worlds_canon.jsonl and results/. These are paid, irreproducible API outputs.\n  - delete: .venv (regenerable with the uv commands), nltk_data (redownloadable) and __pycache__.",
  "fallback_plan": "F0. Iter-1 paths unreadable or code incompatible.\n- Re-implement tvjt.find_world, which is about 50 lines: a z3 Optimize over a ground propositional encoding for domains 1..4, with soft ¬atom constraints for minimality, re-checked by pure-Python evaluation.\n- Re-implement the mutants for the 6 highest-yield operators: NEG, QUANT, IMPL_REV, DROP_CONJ, ADD_CONJ and ARG_SWAP, plus SCOPE_SWAP and CARD, which are needed for the blind-spot question.\n- Log the deviation. If Arm A's A3 cannot be run, drop A3 from stacking and crossover location, keep LC, and report the omission.\n\nF1. No repair configuration reaches paired G2 ≤ 0.10 on the screen. Try these, still screen-only and in this order, and stop at the first that passes:\n(i) C3 plus ABSTAIN-ON-UNSTABLE. Worlds whose 3 votes disagree (majority < 3/3) are dropped from the score. If fewer than 2 worlds remain, the item is uncovered, which is counted.\n(ii) R1b, SEMANTIC BOUNDARY WORLDS, which are fully syntax-independent. Replace the mutant-derived worlds with up to 8 'boundary' worlds:\n- a boundary world is a smallest world w in which flipping ONE atom flips F(w);\n- they are enumerated with z3 over n ≤ 3, ordered by name-blind canonical key, with balanced F-true and F-false;\n- equivalent formulas then get identical world sets by construction.\n(iii) Mean over worlds replaced by the median over operator families.\nFreeze the best configuration anyway, with frozen_G2_pass=false, and state it in the verdict. The held-out crossover test still runs, because its question does not depend on invariance.\n\nF2. RENAME alone breaks G2. Record it as a verbaliser and synonym limitation, and do not tune the verbaliser on held-out.\n\nF3. Budget binds; the tracked cumulative cost crosses a stage soft stop. Cut in this priority order:\n1. Keep P (609) whole for TVJT*, B1, B1x3 and TVJT_iter1.\n2. Keep Ccon for the 3 named systems.\n3. Subsample T BY SENTENCE, stratified by corpus (e.g. 200 of the 316 sentences, keeping all 9 systems per sentence), and state n.\n4. Drop B1x3 on T.\n5. Restrict TVJT_lite to 300 P items.\n6. Skip the held-out G2 replication.\nAt $9.50 the hard breaker stops all calls, and the analysis runs on what exists.\n\nF4. OpenRouter rate limits, or a daily key limit shared with other runs.\n- Use exponential backoff and resume from the cache. Every stage must be resumable.\n- If gemini-2.5-flash is unavailable for more than 45 minutes, finish the zero-LLM parts (LC, A3, B3sc, canon diagnostics).\n- Report the LLM metrics on whatever subset completed, P first. Never switch the TVJT judge model mid-confirmation; a different judge becomes a separately labelled variant.\n\nF5. The syntax bridge loses more than 15% of any system's parseable candidates.\n- Add targeted normalisations to the bridge: equality, ⊕ spelled 'xor', nested function terms rejected.\n- Otherwise report per-system coverage and give AUROC on the jointly covered subset as a co-primary.\n\nF6. The LC pairwise equivalence is too slow (projected over 90 min).\n- Restrict LC to sentences in P ∪ T ∪ Ccon.\n- Reduce the per-pair wall to 5 s.\n- Use the fingerprint pre-filter only, labelling a pair non-equivalent when the truth-vector fingerprints differ, and run z3 only on fingerprint matches.\n\nF7. The panel top-tercile count is below the power threshold. The pre-registered switch applies: evaluate H1 on T with soft labels plus multiple imputation, and treat P-top as secondary. Say plainly that soft-label AUROC inherits the L1-stratum label model.\n\nF8. The judge returns malformed JSON on more than 5% of batches with the compact format. Revert to iter-1's key-value format {'1': 'TRUE', ...} with a separate confidence field. Decide this during the 50-target mini run on the SCREEN, before freezing.",
  "testing_plan": "T0 unit tests (pytest, no API, less than 2 minutes):\n(a) canon() is idempotent, with canon(canon(F)) == canon(F) on 50 screen golds.\n(b) canon(F) is bounded-equivalent to F on all 300 golds.\n(c) A→B, ¬B→¬A, ¬A∨B and ¬(A∧¬B) share one canonical string.\n(d) A synonym-renamed formula has the identical skeleton and canon_seed.\n(e) Conjunct reorder gives an identical canonical string.\n(f) find_world is deterministic: two calls give identical true_atoms.\n(g) The world isomorphism key is invariant under element relabeling.\n(h) The syntax bridge round-trips 20 held-out candidates from different systems (ASCII ^, LaTeX, snake_case) into fol_core.\n(i) The weighted AUROC function matches sklearn roc_auc_score(sample_weight=...) on random data, and the soft AUROC with 0/1 labels equals the hard AUROC.\n(j) The label loader raises an error if freeze_receipt.json is missing or its hash mismatches.\n\nT1 zero-cost canonical diagnostic. Compute the world-set identity rate per rewrite kind on the 499 screen rewrites. The gate is at least 90% for REORDER, DEMORGAN and CONTRAPOSITIVE before any paid call; if it fails, fix canon() first.\n\nT2 cache reproduction ($0).\n- Re-scoring 20 iter-1 screen items with C0 reproduces the iter-1 TVJT scores exactly from the copied cache.\n- Re-scoring 20 items with the exact B1 prompt hits the iter-1 cache.\n- A mismatch means the prompt was not reproduced exactly; fix it before continuing.\n\nT3 mini run on the SCREEN: 50 targets (golds plus their rewrites plus real items), using C1/C2/C3.\n- Check: malformed-JSON rate < 2%; cost per target within 30% of the ≈$0.0012 estimate (3 votes); world-level cache hits > 0.\n- Check that the paired FA on these rewrites is already visibly lower than iter-1's on the same items.\n- Extrapolate the cost of the full screen and of held-out P/T/Ccon, and adjust stage budgets BEFORE scaling.\n\nT4 scale on the screen: 300 → all. Check that AUROC_real stays within noise of iter-1 (.70 ± .05) and that the cost ledger totals match the OpenRouter usage fields. Use a 2000× sentence-clustered bootstrap.\\n- Secondary: AUROC under the screen_gold_audit panel-audited label, joined on sentence_id and system.\\n- Per tercile.\\n- Paired G2 FA: the share of rewrites r with score(r) < score(gold_of_r) − 0.2. Report it overall, for non-RENAME kinds, and per kind.\\n- The iter-1 absolute G2 at τ* (τ* chosen by Youden on the screen) and at 0.5.\\n- Per-operator pairwise detection on gold worlds.\\n- Test-retest (P1b): 150 random screen targets are re-called in a separate cache namespace 'retest'.\\n  - Report per-world verdict agreement with Cohen κ.\\n  - Report the score ICC(2,1) between the two runs for C1 and for C3.\\n  - Report how many worlds have 3-vote disagreement.\\n\\nFREEZE RULE (pre-declared here; do not change after seeing results):\\n- A configuration is ELIGIBLE if (a) its paired G2 FA over ALL rewrites is ≤ 0.10, and (b) its screen AUROC_real(L_bij) is ≥ 0.701 − 0.02 = 0.681.\\n- Pick the eligible configuration with the highest AUROC_real. If two are within 0.01, pick the one with fewer calls: C1 < C2 = C3, then prefer C3 for threshold-freeness.\\n- If none is eligible only because of RENAME (non-RENAME FA ≤ 0.10), pick by the same AUROC rule and record 'G2_all_failed_by_RENAME'. The RENAME FA is then reported as the residual verbaliser/synonym limitation.\\n- If none is eligible at all, go to FALLBACK F1/F2, still on the screen only. Freeze the best configuration anyway, with frozen_G2_pass=false. The crossover question is tested regardless.\\n- Choose the binary threshold τ_frozen by Youden on the screen real items. It is used only for the binary G2 and detection rates.\\n\\nWrite results/frozen_config.json:\\n- config id, prompt text and version, model, T=0, the vote scheme, the scoring formula, τ_frozen and the canon() options;\\n- git hash or sha1 of src/*.py;\\n- the screen metrics that justified it.\\nWrite results/prereg.json with every PHASE-2 hypothesis, test, bin edge and decision rule listed in section 7. Hash both files into results/freeze_receipt.json, with a UTC timestamp. Only then may the held-out LABEL loader run: src/labels.py asserts that freeze_receipt.json exists and that its hashes match.\\nThe held-out SCORING code reads ONLY the input, metadata_item_id, metadata_sentence_id, metadata_system, metadata_corpus and complexity fields. It never reads output or metadata_L3_* fields.\\n\\n=== 5. PHASE 2: HELD-OUT SCORING (label-blind) ===\\nLoad full_data_out_*.json. Sort the parts numerically, following the dataset README loader.\\n\\nItem sets:\\n- P = heldout_confirm rows with metadata_label_source=='panel3' (609).\\n- T = heldout_confirm rows in the top tercile. Check the encoding of metadata_complexity_tercile (0/1/2 or labels) from label_report.json; the top tercile is the highest-composite one. Expect about 316 sentences × 9 = 2,844 rows, all systems, including unparseable ones.\\n- Ccon = contamination rows for systems {gpt-4.1-mini, deepseek-v3.1, llama-3.1-8b}, each paired with its original heldout_confirm row via metadata_original_item_id. That gives 312 pairs, or 624 targets. Extend to all 9 systems if budget remains after P2b.\\n- S_all = all 6,300 heldout_confirm rows. These are needed ONLY for the zero-LLM LC fit and for B3sc.\\n\\nMetrics on P ∪ T ∪ Ccon, identical items throughout:\\n- TVJT* (frozen), with all 3 votes.\\n- TVJT_iter1: the unrepaired iter-1 code (seed=item_id, iter-1 prompt, 1 vote), on P and T only. This is the within-artifact ablation.\\n- B1: the EXACT iter-1 fixed prompt from results/b1_request_example.json and prompts_frozen.json, on gemini-2.5-flash at T=0 with reasoning off, 1 call, output a 0-100 probability.\\n- B1x3: the same prompt with 3 calls. Vote_idx 1 and 2 are sent with the identical prompt and a cache bypass; take the mean of the 3. Run it on P and on T if budget allows. This is the COMPUTE-MATCHED judge.\\n- TVJT_lite: the frozen configuration with google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite as judge, on P only.\\n- parse_ok, both as metadata_parse_ok and as fol_core-bridge coverage.\\n- LC_onecoin (zero-LLM):\\n  - For every sentence in S_all, compute pairwise fc.find_bijections(a_i, a_j)['label'] among its 9 parseable candidates. That is up to 36 pairs × 700 sentences, run on a ProcessPool with 4 workers, spawn, wall 10 s per pair; a timeout counts as not-equivalent and is logged.\\n  - Then run latent_class.run_latent_class (the one-coin EM with an all-wrong state) over all 700 sentences, giving a posterior per candidate.\\n- A3: third_party/armA method.signature_faithfulness(sentence, bridged_fol_str, variant='A3'), on P ∪ T.\\n- B3sc (zero-LLM, standard self-consistency baseline):\\n  - For gpt-4.1-mini and llama-3.1-8b greedy rows, take the share of that system's 5 heldout_samples for the same sentence that are find_bijections-equivalent to the greedy candidate.\\n  - Available on 2/9 systems only; report it on that subset.\\nPersist everything to results/heldout_scores.jsonl, one row per (item_id, metric), with score, covered, error_type_pred, n_worlds, usd, seconds, canon_fallback and bridge.\\n\\nOPTIONAL, if budget remains (P2d): held-out G2 replication, which needs no labels.\\n- Take 100 held-out audited golds (metadata_gold_fol_audited not null, sentence-stratified by corpus).\\n- For each, run mutants.make_rewrites(k=2), which gives about 200 rewrites. Score the golds and the rewrites with TVJT*.\\n- Report the paired FA per kind. Expected cost is about $0.2.\\n\\n=== 6. LABELS (src/labels.py; runs only after freeze_receipt) ===\\n- y_panel (P): metadata_L3_majority mapped to 1 = faithful, with weight w = metadata_L3_sampling_weight.\\n- y_hard (T and other rows): output in {faithful, unfaithful}. unknown is excluded from y_hard metrics and COUNTED.\\n- y_soft:\\n  - panel rows: y_panel;\\n  - otherwise p_faithful = metadata_L3_stratum_p_faithful when present;\\n  - else 0.90 for L1-equivalent rows and 0.44 for L1-non-equivalent rows, from label_report.json (9.9% of equivalent rows are unfaithful; 43.9% of non-equivalent rows are faithful).\\n- Contamination: the output label transferred to the renamed row. Rows with metadata_contamination_rename_incomplete are flagged and reported with and without them.\\n\\n=== 7. ANALYSES (src/analysis2.py; every number goes to results/analysis.json) ===\\nA. Weighted AUROC on P for every metric:\\n   AUC_w = Σ_{i∈pos, j∈neg} w_i w_j [1(s_i>s_j) + .5·1(s_i=s_j)] / (Σw_pos · Σw_neg)\\n   - CI: 2000× cluster bootstrap that resamples sentence_ids within corpus strata (seed 0), reusing the SAME resamples for all metrics so the deltas are paired.\\n   - Also report the unweighted AUROC and the within-sentence pairwise accuracy (a faithful candidate outscores an unfaithful candidate of the same sentence, ties 0.5), over sentences with mixed panel or L1-equivalent labels.\\n   - Also report every AUROC excluding gemini-2.5-flash-generated candidates, because the judge model is also a generator.\\nB. PRE-REGISTERED CROSSOVER (the primary question). Bins: complexity tercile (bottom/mid/top) and n_conditions bins {0-1, 2-3, 4+}.\\n   - H1 (primary): on P restricted to the top tercile, ΔAUROC_w(TVJT* − B1) > 0, with the paired-bootstrap 95% CI lower bound > 0.\\n     Power switch, decided BEFORE labels are read, from label-free counts: if the top-tercile panel count is < 150 or has fewer than 40 of either class-agnostic... (use the count only), H1 is evaluated on T with y_soft (soft AUROC, below), and P-top becomes secondary.\\n   - H2 (growth): Δ_top − Δ_bottom > 0 (paired bootstrap CI), and the Δ per n_conditions bin is monotone non-decreasing (reported with CIs).\\n   - H2b (logistic interaction):\\n     - Per metric m, fit a weighted GLM (statsmodels, Binomial, freq_weights = w, cluster-robust SE by sentence_id): y ~ z(s_m) + c + z(s_m):c, with c = complexity_composite (and, separately, n_conditions).\\n     - Report the interaction slope β3 with its CI.\\n     - Joint model: y ~ z(s_TVJT)·c + z(s_B1)·c. Test β(TVJT:c) − β(B1:c) > 0 with a bootstrap CI.\\n   - Soft-label AUROC on T: each item contributes as a positive with weight p and as a negative with weight 1−p (probabilistic ROC).\\n     - Also run multiple imputation: 200 Bernoulli(p) label draws, AUROC per draw, report the mean and the 2.5–97.5% interval, pooled with the cluster bootstrap.\\n     - Also report the hard-label (y_hard) AUROC on T, flagged as biased toward gold vocabulary.\\n   - Repeat B for LC_onecoin, A3, B1x3, TVJT_iter1 and TVJT_lite, to locate which component carries the long-sentence regime.\\n     Key contrasts: TVJT* − B1x3 (compute-matched) and TVJT* − TVJT_iter1 (did the repair cost signal?).\\nC. REAL ERROR TYPES (P, unfaithful items grouped by metadata_L3_primary_error).\\n   - Detection for type e = P(score of the erroneous item < score of a faithful candidate for the SAME sentence). Faithful comparators are panel-faithful or L1-equivalent candidates. Ties count 0.5.\\n   - Fallback when a sentence has no faithful comparator: compare against all panel-faithful items (the per-type AUROC).\\n   - Types: quantifier_scope, cardinality, quantifier_forall_exists, added_condition, dropped_condition, and/or (use the dataset's exact strings), plus implication_direction_or_only.\\n   - Report n, detection and Wilson or bootstrap CIs for TVJT*, B1, A3 and LC.\\n   - Head-to-head on {quantifier_scope ∪ cardinality}: TVJT* − A3, where A3 is predicted near 0.5.\\n   - Supplement with the screen blindspot_supplement: SCOPE_SWAP n=37 and CARD, pairwise detection on gold worlds under the frozen protocol.\\n   - Error-type IDENTIFICATION: map TVJT error_type operator families to L3 types (NEG→negation, QUANT→forall_exists, SCOPE_SWAP→scope, CARD→cardinality, DROP_CONJ→dropped, ADD_CONJ→added, IMPL_REV→implication, AND_OR→and_or, ARG_SWAP→swapped_args, MERGE→conflation). Report the confusion matrix and macro-F1; this is secondary.\\nD. STACKING on P (and on T with y_hard as secondary):\\n   - Cross-fitted logistic regression (GroupKFold by sentence_id, 5 folds, sample weights w, standardized features, C=1.0, fixed).\\n   - Feature sets:\\n     - S0 = [B1, parse_ok]\\n     - S1 = S0 + TVJT*\\n     - S2 = S1 + LC + A3\\n     - S0z = S0 + LC + A3 (the zero-LLM stack without TVJT)\\n   - Report the OOF AUROC_w and the paired-bootstrap ΔAUROC for S1−S0, S2−S0z (TVJT's marginal value over the full gold-free stack) and S2−S0.\\n   - Placebo: S0 + a shuffled TVJT column, whose Δ CI must contain 0.\\nE. CONTAMINATION (Ccon): per metric, AUROC on the originals vs the paraphrases (paired bootstrap over the 104 pairs), the mean score shift orig→para per label class, and |Δ|.\\n   The prediction is TVJT Δ≈0 and B1 possibly negative. Report with and without rename_incomplete rows.\\nF. SYSTEM LEVEL: Kendall τ between each system's mean metric score and its accuracy.\\n   - Accuracy on P is w-weighted; the y_soft accuracy uses all heldout_confirm rows.\\n   - Bootstrap CI over sentences. Note that with 9 systems |τ| ≥ 0.61 is needed for p < .05.\\nG. COST CURVE: $/item (fresh-call cost amortised over cache reuse, and also the no-cache cost) and median seconds per item.\\n   Plot AUROC_w on P against $/item for B1, B1x3, TVJT_lite, TVJT* with 1 vote (C1-equivalent from vote 0) and TVJT* with 3 votes.\\nH. COVERAGE: bridge failures, no-world rate, malformed-JSON rate and canon_fallback rate, per system and corpus. Verbaliser failure rate counted as in iter 1.\\nI. Figures (aii-data-fig-gen):\\n   (1) the AUROC by tercile / n_conditions bin line plot for TVJT*, B1, B1x3, LC and A3, with CIs;\\n   (2) the ΔAUROC(TVJT*−B1) forest plot by bin;\\n   (3) the paired-FA per rewrite kind, iter-1 vs frozen;\\n   (4) the cost-accuracy scatter;\\n   (5) the real-error-type detection bars.\\n\\nDECISION TABLE (written into prereg.json and applied verbatim in results/verdict.json):\\n- CROSSOVER_CONFIRMED: H1 lower bound > 0 AND H2 Δ_top − Δ_bottom lower bound > 0.\\n- CROSSOVER_PARTIAL: H1 holds, H2 is not significant.\\n- NOT_REPLICATED: the H1 CI includes 0.\\n- REVERSED: the H1 upper bound < 0.\\n- Separately:\\n  - REPAIR_OK if the frozen screen G2 passed and the held-out G2 replication (if run) has paired FA ≤ 0.10;\\n  - JUDGE_MATCHED if TVJT* − B1x3 in the top tercile has a lower bound > 0 (otherwise the advantage may be vote averaging);\\n  - STACK_INCREMENT if the S2 − S0z lower bound > 0.\\n\\n=== 8. OUTPUTS ===\\n- method.py: the orchestrator, run as --stage setup|canon_diag|repair|freeze|score_heldout|labels|analysis|export [--limit N].\\n- src/metrics_api2.py exposes the reusable functions:\\n  - tvjt_score(text, fol, config='frozen') -> {score, covered, error_type, worlds, agrees, usd}\\n  - canon(fol_str) -> str\\n  - distinguishing_worlds(fol_str) -> list\\n  Docstrings state precisely what each one measures.\\n- method_out.json in the exp_gen_sol_out schema, validated with aii-json. It holds a dataset per group: screen_real, screen_rewrites, heldout_panel, heldout_top, contamination. Each example has input, output (label), predict_TVJT_frozen, predict_TVJT_iter1, predict_B1, predict_B1x3, predict_TVJT_lite, predict_LC, predict_A3 and predict_B3sc (as strings), plus metadata_* fields.\\n  If it exceeds the limit, split it with aii-file-size-limit, and create the mini and preview variants.\\n- results/: frozen_config.json, prereg.json, freeze_receipt.json, analysis.json, verdict.json, heldout_scores.jsonl, screen_repair_scores.jsonl, cost_ledger.jsonl.\\n- README.md and .aii/manifest.yaml:\\n  - keep: cache/llm*, data/worlds_canon.jsonl and results/. These are paid, irreproducible API outputs.\\n  - delete: .venv (regenerable with the uv commands), nltk_data (redownloadable) and __pycache__.\",\n  \"fallback_plan\": \"F0. Iter-1 paths unreadable or code incompatible.\\n- Re-implement tvjt.find_world, which is about 50 lines: a z3 Optimize over a ground propositional encoding for domains 1..4, with soft ¬atom constraints for minimality, re-checked by pure-Python evaluation.\\n- Re-implement the mutants for the 6 highest-yield operators: NEG, QUANT, IMPL_REV, DROP_CONJ, ADD_CONJ and ARG_SWAP, plus SCOPE_SWAP and CARD, which are needed for the blind-spot question.\\n- Log the deviation. If Arm A's A3 cannot be run, drop A3 from stacking and crossover location, keep LC, and report the omission.\\n\\nF1. No repair configuration reaches paired G2 ≤ 0.10 on the screen. Try these, still screen-only and in this order, and stop at the first that passes:\\n(i) C3 plus ABSTAIN-ON-UNSTABLE. Worlds whose 3 votes disagree (majority < 3/3) are dropped from the score. If fewer than 2 worlds remain, the item is uncovered, which is counted.\\n(ii) R1b, SEMANTIC BOUNDARY WORLDS, which are fully syntax-independent. Replace the mutant-derived worlds with up to 8 'boundary' worlds:\\n- a boundary world is a smallest world w in which flipping ONE atom flips F(w);\\n- they are enumerated with z3 over n ≤ 3, ordered by name-blind canonical key, with balanced F-true and F-false;\\n- equivalent formulas then get identical world sets by construction.\\n(iii) Mean over worlds replaced by the median over operator families.\\nFreeze the best configuration anyway, with frozen_G2_pass=false, and state it in the verdict. The held-out crossover test still runs, because its question does not depend on invariance.\\n\\nF2. RENAME alone breaks G2. Record it as a verbaliser and synonym limitation, and do not tune the verbaliser on held-out.\\n\\nF3. Budget binds; the tracked cumulative cost crosses a stage soft stop. Cut in this priority order:\\n1. Keep P (609) whole for TVJT*, B1, B1x3 and TVJT_iter1.\\n2. Keep Ccon for the 3 named systems.\\n3. Subsample T BY SENTENCE, stratified by corpus (e.g. 200 of the 316 sentences, keeping all 9 systems per sentence), and state n.\\n4. Drop B1x3 on T.\\n5. Restrict TVJT_lite to 300 P items.\\n6. Skip the held-out G2 replication.\\nAt $9.50 the hard breaker stops all calls, and the analysis runs on what exists.\\n\\nF4. OpenRouter rate limits, or a daily key limit shared with other runs.\\n- Use exponential backoff and resume from the cache. Every stage must be resumable.\\n- If gemini-2.5-flash is unavailable for more than 45 minutes, finish the zero-LLM parts (LC, A3, B3sc, canon diagnostics).\\n- Report the LLM metrics on whatever subset completed, P first. Never switch the TVJT judge model mid-confirmation; a different judge becomes a separately labelled variant.\\n\\nF5. The syntax bridge loses more than 15% of any system's parseable candidates.\\n- Add targeted normalisations to the bridge: equality, ⊕ spelled 'xor', nested function terms rejected.\\n- Otherwise report per-system coverage and give AUROC on the jointly covered subset as a co-primary.\\n\\nF6. The LC pairwise equivalence is too slow (projected over 90 min).\\n- Restrict LC to sentences in P ∪ T ∪ Ccon.\\n- Reduce the per-pair wall to 5 s.\\n- Use the fingerprint pre-filter only, labelling a pair non-equivalent when the truth-vector fingerprints differ, and run z3 only on fingerprint matches.\\n\\nF7. The panel top-tercile count is below the power threshold. The pre-registered switch applies: evaluate H1 on T with soft labels plus multiple imputation, and treat P-top as secondary. Say plainly that soft-label AUROC inherits the L1-stratum label model.\\n\\nF8. The judge returns malformed JSON on more than 5% of batches with the compact format. Revert to iter-1's key-value format {'1': 'TRUE', ...} with a separate confidence field. Decide this during the 50-target mini run on the SCREEN, before freezing.\",\n  \"testing_plan\": \"T0 unit tests (pytest, no API, less than 2 minutes):\\n(a) canon() is idempotent, with canon(canon(F)) == canon(F) on 50 screen golds.\\n(b) canon(F) is bounded-equivalent to F on all 300 golds.\\n(c) A→B, ¬B→¬A, ¬A∨B and ¬(A∧¬B) share one canonical string.\\n(d) A synonym-renamed formula has the identical skeleton and canon_seed.\\n(e) Conjunct reorder gives an identical canonical string.\\n(f) find_world is deterministic: two calls give identical true_atoms.\\n(g) The world isomorphism key is invariant under element relabeling.\\n(h) The syntax bridge round-trips 20 held-out candidates from different systems (ASCII ^, LaTeX, snake_case) into fol_core.\\n(i) The weighted AUROC function matches sklearn roc_auc_score(sample_weight=...) on random data, and the soft AUROC with 0/1 labels equals the hard AUROC.\\n(j) The label loader raises an error if freeze_receipt.json is missing or its hash mismatches.\\n\\nT1 zero-cost canonical diagnostic. Compute the world-set identity rate per rewrite kind on the 499 screen rewrites. The gate is at least 90% for REORDER, DEMORGAN and CONTRAPOSITIVE before any paid call; if it fails, fix canon() first.\\n\\nT2 cache reproduction ($0).\\n- Re-scoring 20 iter-1 screen items with C0 reproduces the iter-1 TVJT scores exactly from the copied cache.\\n- Re-scoring 20 items with the exact B1 prompt hits the iter-1 cache.\\n- A mismatch means the prompt was not reproduced exactly; fix it before continuing.\\n\\nT3 mini run on the SCREEN: 50 targets (golds plus their rewrites plus real items), using C1/C2/C3.\\n- Check: malformed-JSON rate < 2%; cost per target within 30% of the ≈$0.0012 estimate (3 votes); world-level cache hits > 0.\\n- Check that the paired FA on these rewrites is already visibly lower than iter-1's on the same items.\\n- Extrapolate the cost of the full screen and of held-out P/T/Ccon, and adjust stage budgets BEFORE scaling.\\n\\nT4 scale on the screen: 300 → all. Check that AUROC_real stays within noise of iter-1 (.70 ± .05) and that the cost ledger totals match the OpenRouter usage fields. Then run the freeze and write the receipt.\\n\\nT5 held-out mini, label-blind: 50 P items. Check bridge coverage per system, world coverage, B1 parse rate of the 0-100 outputs and seconds per item. Then scale P → Ccon → T in stages of 300 and re-extrapolate cost at each stage.\\n\\nT6 analysis sanity:\\n- Shuffled labels give AUROC ≈ 0.5.\\n- A random-noise metric gives a stacking Δ whose CI contains 0.\\n- The weighted AUROC of parse_ok on P is reported, as a known weak reference.\\n- Recomputing the P AUROC with the unweighted formula gives a plausible difference, below 0.05; flag it if larger.\\n- An independent re-derivation script (audit/rederive.py, a different code path: sklearn plus a pandas bootstrap) recomputes the headline numbers from heldout_scores.jsonl and the labels:\\n  - H1 Δ, the per-tercile AUROCs, the stack Δs and the G2 FA.\\n  - Point estimates must match exactly; CIs to the second decimal.\\n\\nT7 the final checks:\\n- method_out.json validates against exp_gen_sol_out.\\n- File sizes are checked.\\n- The README lists every number's source JSON path.\\n- The manifest covers .venv and nltk_data.\\n\\nThroughout, log the cumulative $ after every stage, and abort a stage whose running cost exceeds 1.3× its extrapolation.\",\n  \"domain_practice\": \"What was read:\\n- the aii neuro-symbolic handbook (mid-2026), its critical rules and its eval rows;\\n- this run's iter-1 artifacts: the Arm B README with results, gates and per-operator tables, and the dataset README with label_report facts;\\n- Thatikonda, Buntine and Shareghi, 'Assessing the Sensitivity and Alignment of FOL Closeness Metrics' (EMNLP Findings 2025, aclanthology 2025.findings-emnlp.910). It uses operator and text perturbations of gold FOL as a sensitivity instrument, and measures alignment with an LLM judge;\\n- the DPC / Minimal Distinguishing Database abstract (arXiv:2604.15163). It uses distinguishing micro-environments judged by an agent, compared against Self-Consistency and LLM-as-a-Judge on BIRD/Spider;\\n- the strategy's field summary (Zhong et al. 2020 test suites; arXiv:2606.02837 relabelling; round-trip 2604.25031; Monty 2607.13303).\\n\\nBASELINES in every comparable paper:\\n(1) LLM-as-a-judge on the candidate. This is the baseline a reviewer names first. The fair way to tune it is the same model family as the proposed judge, a fixed prompt frozen before test, AND matched compute: if the proposed method spends k calls, the judge gets k samples or self-consistency.\\n(2) Sampling self-consistency over the generator's own samples (2410.20936; DPC's main competitor).\\n(3) Round-trip / back-translation (2604.25031) and clause conformance (Monty).\\n(4) Parse or compile rate.\\nFor distinguishing-input methods specifically (LLMCodeChoice, DPC), the head-to-head is always against the judge and self-consistency on IDENTICAL candidate pools.\\n\\nDATA:\\n- FOLIO and MALLS are standard but ~36–62% of the shipped gold is wrong. The accepted remedy is corrected or audited labels (2606.02837, DSAVlab-UNIUD HF release) or adjudication.\\n- ProverQA is the contamination-resistant, prover-synthesized option, although this run found 17.5% of its gold drops restrictors.\\n- FOLIO-dev short sentences are saturated for judges.\\n- Exception-rich ('unless/except') sentences are essentially absent from public gold, so 'heavily conditioned' has to be operationalised as connective, ⊕/↔ and nesting load.\\n\\nCONTROLS:\\n- same items;\\n- same candidates;\\n- the same label source, family-disjoint from judge and generators;\\n- the same judge model when comparing input formats;\\n- frozen prompts and thresholds before test labels;\\n- unparseable outputs counted, not dropped;\\n- sentence-clustered resampling, because 9 system outputs per sentence are not independent;\\n- post-stratified weights when adjudication oversampled strata.\\nThe confound most likely to be caught here is that a gain comes from extra samples, i.e. vote averaging, rather than from the input format, or that it comes from the judge having generated some of the candidates.\\n\\nHOW MUCH IS ENOUGH:\\n- Item-level meta-evaluations report hundreds to low thousands of labelled items.\\n- For an AUROC near .7 with about 40% positives, n≈600 gives a CI half-width of about ±.04–.05, and n≈280 about ±.10. Iter-1's top-tercile CI was [+.085, +.308].\\n- So the 609-item panel can only resolve top-tercile gaps of about ≥ .10, and more graded items, not more metrics, is the fix: here, about 2.8k top-tercile candidates with probabilistic labels.\\n- System-level Kendall τ with 9 systems needs |τ| ≥ .61 for significance, so it is reported but weak.\\n- Error-type detection on rare real types (scope and cardinality, 6.9%) will have n≈20–30, so Wilson CIs are mandatory.\\n\\nMEASURES AND REPORTING:\\n- threshold-free AUROC, weighted where the design requires, with clustered bootstrap CIs;\\n- paired ΔAUROC on identical items;\\n- within-sentence pairwise accuracy;\\n- cross-fitted stacking increments (ΔAUROC over the baseline stack);\\n- per-error-type detection;\\n- false-alarm rate on solver-verified meaning-preserving rewrites (Thatikonda-style typed perturbations as a mechanism and invariance instrument, never as the faithfulness target);\\n- coverage;\\n- $/item and s/item;\\n- complexity-stratified tables.\",\n  \"practice_alignment\": \"MEETS:\\n(1) Identical items for every metric. The judge baseline is the EXACT frozen iter-1 prompt, and a compute-matched B1x3 was ADDED so that the 3-vote TVJT is not compared against a 1-sample judge. This closes the gap a reviewer would name first.\\n(2) Self-consistency (B3sc) was ADDED at zero cost from heldout_samples. Parse rate is included.\\n(3) Labels are family-disjoint from the gemini judge and the generators (haiku/grok/glm panel), with post-stratified weights, and unparseable outputs are counted.\\n(4) All tuning happens on the screen. frozen_config, prereg and a hash receipt are written before the held-out label loader can run, and the scoring code is label-blind by construction.\\n(5) Stratified, sentence-clustered paired bootstrap, and the complexity-stratified reporting that the user asked for.\\n(6) Perturbations and rewrites are used only as invariance and mechanism instruments. The faithfulness target is the adjudicated label.\\n(7) Contamination is probed with an entity-renamed paraphrase fold.\\n(8) Power: the crossover is powered by adding about 2.8k top-tercile items, rather than by adding more metrics.\\n\\nDEPARTS:\\n(a) The labels are an LLM panel, not humans (κ=.57 vs the human MALLS corrections; Fleiss κ .63). Budget and scope justify this: the dataset artifact owns the labels. Cost: absolute AUROCs carry panel noise, which attenuates everything. The paired Δs are less affected.\\n(b) The top-tercile expansion uses solver labels (biased toward gold vocabulary) and soft stratum labels (model-based). Cost: if H1 is decided on T, it inherits the stratum label model. Mitigations: the switch rule is fixed on label-free counts, results are reported under hard, soft and multiply-imputed labels, and P-top is always shown.\\n(c) Round-trip and Monty conformance are NOT re-run here. They belong to the parallel iter-2 frozen-confirmation experiment (dir1), which shares the B1 prompt and item_ids. Cost: TVJT's increment is shown over [B1, parse, LC, A3, self-consistency], not over round-trip. If dir1's per-item scores exist when the analysis runs, join them by metadata_item_id and add them as a secondary stack; otherwise state the omission.\\n(d) The judge is a single model family (gemini-2.5-flash, kept for comparability with iter 1), and gemini-2.5-flash is also one of the 9 generators. This is mitigated by reporting every AUROC with gemini-generated candidates excluded, and by the flash-lite variant. Cost: no claim about judges from other families.\\n(e) 'Conditions' means connectives, ⊕/↔ and nesting, not legal exceptions. Public gold has almost none of the latter. Cost: the user's EU-AI-Act regime is only approximated, and this is stated plainly.\\n(f) Self-consistency covers 2 of 9 systems, because samples exist only for those. It is reported on that subset.\\n(g) The system-level τ with 9 systems is underpowered. It is reported descriptively.\\n(h) The rare real error types (scope and cardinality, about 7%) cannot give tight per-type estimates. The screen SCOPE_SWAP supplement (n=37) is synthetic. Cost: the 'TVJT sees what the signature cannot' claim rests mainly on mechanism evidence, with real-error support at low n and wide CIs.\\n(i) The gloss verbalisation option is unavailable on held-out, because there are no predicate glosses. The frozen configuration therefore uses camelCase-split only, even if gloss looked better on the screen.\"\n}\n", "structuredPatch": [], "originalFile": null, "userModified": false}

R1: CANONICAL FORM + canonical mutants/worlds (src/canon.py) ===\\ndef canon(F):\\n  1. Eliminate ↔ and ⊕ only when they sit under a negation; otherwise keep them, to limit blow-up.\\n  2. NNF: push ¬ to atoms. ¬∀ becomes ∃¬ and ¬∃ becomes ∀¬; De Morgan is applied; ¬(A→B) becomes A∧¬B.\\n  3. Restricted-implication normalisation: every implication A→B, and every disjunction directly under ∀ that contains negative literals, is rewritten as (conjunction of the POSITIVE forms of the negative disjuncts) → (disjunction of the remaining disjuncts).\\n     - So A→B, ¬B→¬A and ¬A∨B all map to one form. This kills the CONTRAPOSITIVE and DEMORGAN false alarms.\\n     - If every disjunct is negative, the consequent is the ¬literal that comes last in skeleton order.\\n  4. Prenex with mutants.prenex. Keep the original if prenexing fails (NotPrenexable), and set the flag.\\n  5. Flatten ∧ and ∨ and sort their operands by the NAME-BLIND skeleton string: predicates replaced by 'P/arity', constants by 'c', variables alpha-renamed. Ties are broken by the full string.\\n  6. Alpha-rename bound variables to x, y, z, w, … in order of first occurrence.\\n  7. Size guard: if the canonical tree has more than 3× the original node count, use the original with canon_fallback=True.\\n  8. Verify the canonical form: bounded_equiv(canon(F), F, nmax=4) must be 'equiv'. Otherwise fall back to the original and set the flag.\\n  canon_seed = sha1(skeleton string of canon(F)). This is name-blind, so a RENAME rewrite gets the same seed.\\n\\ndef canonical_worlds(F):\\n  Fc = canon(F)\\n  For op in OPERATORS (CARD with variants 'two' and 'unique', as iter-1 card_variants=True):\\n      m = make_mutant(Fc, op, canon_seed)          # canonical seed, never item_id\\n  Deduplicate the kept mutants by pairwise bounded_equiv; keep the first in OPERATORS order.\\n  For each distinct mutant: w = find_world(Fc, m, prefer=0 fixed, nmax=4), with z3.set_param('smt.random_seed', 0) and 'sat.random_seed' 0.\\n  Deduplicate worlds by isomorphism: canonical key = sorted true_atoms after relabeling elements in order of first appearance. Take the lexicographic minimum over the ≤4! element permutations.\\n  Cap at 12 worlds. Record per world: op, F_value, world_key, and n.\\n  Order the worlds canonically by world_key.\\nCACHE: data/worlds_canon.jsonl, keyed by sha1(to_str(Fc)). Identical canonical forms across systems and rewrites reuse the worlds.\\n\\nZERO-COST DIAGNOSTIC, run BEFORE any LLM call: for each of the 499 screen rewrites, check whether it has the same world multiset as its source gold, up to predicate renaming for RENAME.\\n- Report the 'world-set identity rate' per rewrite kind.\\n- Expected: REORDER, DEMORGAN and CONTRAPOSITIVE at 90% or more. If a kind is below 70%, debug canon() before spending any money.\\n\\n=== 3. R2+R3: JUDGE PROTOCOL (src/judge.py) ===\\nPrompt 'tvjt_v2_conf' is iter-1 TVJT_PROMPT with the SAME HEADER and verbaliser. The answer instruction becomes:\\n  'Answer with JSON only, one entry per situation: {\\\"1\\\": [v, c], ...} where v is 1 if the sentence is TRUE in that situation and 0 if FALSE, and c is your confidence 0-100.'\\nThis compact format keeps output tokens low.\\n\\nWorld-level cache:\\n- key_w = sha1(model | prompt_version | sentence | world_text | vote_idx).\\n- For a target, gather its worlds W in canonical order and drop those already cached for vote k.\\n- Send the uncached ones as ONE batched call, with situations presented in order perm_k(U).\\n  - perm_0 = canonical order, perm_1 = reversed, perm_2 = rotated by ceil(|U|/2).\\n  - The situation labels 1..|U| follow the presented order. Answers are mapped back to worlds and stored per (world, k).\\n- A malformed answer gets one retry with a 'JSON only' reminder. If it still fails, those worlds are uncovered for that vote.\\n\\nScore definitions per target:\\n  p_k(w) = c/100 if v==1 else 1 − c/100          # judged P(sentence TRUE in w)\\n  agree_bin_k(w) = 1[v_k==F_value(w)]\\n  C1 = mean_w agree_bin_0(w)                      # canonical worlds, 1 vote\\n  C2 = mean_w 1[majority_k v_k == F_value(w)]     # 3-vote majority\\n  C3 = mean_w (p̄(w) if F_value(w) else 1 − p̄(w)),  p̄ = mean_k p_k(w)   # continuous, confidence-weighted (PRE-SELECTED default)\\n  error_type = the operator family with the lowest mean agreement (ties broken by OPERATORS order), or 'none' if all agreements are ≥ 0.5.\\nOne call serves C1, C2 and C3, so the grid costs 3 calls per uncached world-set and no more.\\n\\n=== 4. PHASE 1: SCREEN REPAIR (labels allowed = screen only) ===\\nTargets:\\n- 833 real screen candidates;\\n- the 300 golds;\\n- the 499 rewrites.\\nWith the world cache, expect about 1.6k targets and about 900 distinct world-sets.\\n\\nConfigurations:\\n- C0 = iter-1 TVJT, from the copied screen_scores and cache ($0).\\n- C0' = the iter-1 prompt on canonical worlds, vote 0. Run only on the 300 golds and 499 rewrites (≈$0.15). This isolates the R1 effect from the prompt change.\\n- C1, C2, C3 as defined in section 3.\\nScale-up: 50 targets → 300 → all. At each step, check cost and time per target and extrapolate against the stage budget.\\n\\nMetrics per configuration:\\n- AUROC_real under L_bij, which is the iter-1 label and gives a like-for-like comparison with .701. LABELS (src/labels.py; runs only after freeze_receipt) ===\\n- y_panel (P): metadata_L3_majority mapped to 1 = faithful, with weight w = metadata_L3_sampling_weight.\\n- y_hard (T and other rows): output in {faithful, unfaithful}. unknown is excluded from y_hard metrics and COUNTED.\\n- y_soft:\\n  - panel rows: y_panel;\\n  - otherwise p_faithful = metadata_L3_stratum_p_faithful when present;\\n  - else 0.90 for L1-equivalent rows and 0.44 for L1-non-equivalent rows, from label_report.json (9.9% of equivalent rows are unfaithful; 43.9% of non-equivalent rows are faithful).\\n- Contamination: the output label transferred to the renamed row.
