Weighted AUROC on P for every metric:\\n   AUC_w = Σ_{i∈pos, j∈neg} w_i w_j [1(s_i>s_j) + .5·1(s_i=s_j)] / (Σw_pos · Σw_neg)\\n   - CI: 2000× cluster bootstrap that resamples sentence_ids within corpus strata (seed 0), reusing the SAME resamples for all metrics so the deltas are paired.\\n   - Also report the unweighted AUROC and the within-sentence pairwise accuracy (a faithful candidate outscores an unfaithful candidate of the same sentence, ties 0.5), over sentences with mixed panel or L1-equivalent labels.\\n   - Also report every AUROC excluding gemini-2.5-flash-generated candidates, because the judge model is also a generator.\\nB. PRE-REGISTERED CROSSOVER (the primary question). Bins: complexity tercile (bottom/mid/top) and n_conditions bins {0-1, 2-3, 4+}.\\n   - H1 (primary): on P restricted to the top tercile, ΔAUROC_w(TVJT* − B1) > 0, with the paired-bootstrap 95% CI lower bound > 0.\\n     Power switch, decided BEFORE labels are read, from label-free counts: if the top-tercile panel count is < 150 or has fewer than 40 of either class-agnostic... (use the count only), H1 is evaluated on T with y_soft (soft AUROC, below), and P-top becomes secondary.\\n   - H2 (growth): Δ_top − Δ_bottom > 0 (paired bootstrap CI), and the Δ per n_conditions bin is monotone non-decreasing (reported with CIs).\\n   - H2b (logistic interaction):\\n     - Per metric m, fit a weighted GLM (statsmodels, Binomial, freq_weights = w, cluster-robust SE by sentence_id): y ~ z(s_m) + c + z(s_m):c, with c = complexity_composite (and, separately, n_conditions).\\n     - Report the interaction slope β3 with its CI.\\n     - Joint model: y ~ z(s_TVJT)·c + z(s_B1)·c. Test β(TVJT:c) − β(B1:c) > 0 with a bootstrap CI.\\n   - Soft-label AUROC on T: each item contributes as a positive with weight p and as a negative with weight 1−p (probabilistic ROC).\\n     - Also run multiple imputation: 200 Bernoulli(p) label draws, AUROC per draw, report the mean and the 2.5–97.5% interval, pooled with the cluster bootstrap.\\n     - Also report the hard-label (y_hard) AUROC on T, flagged as biased toward gold vocabulary.\\n   - Repeat B for LC_onecoin, A3, B1x3, TVJT_iter1 and TVJT_lite, to locate which component carries the long-sentence regime.\\n     Key contrasts: TVJT* − B1x3 (compute-matched) and TVJT* − TVJT_iter1 (did the repair cost signal?).\\nC. REAL ERROR TYPES (P, unfaithful items grouped by metadata_L3_primary_error).\\n   - Detection for type e = P(score of the erroneous item < score of a faithful candidate for the SAME sentence). Faithful comparators are panel-faithful or L1-equivalent candidates. Ties count 0.5.\\n   - Fallback when a sentence has no faithful comparator: compare against all panel-faithful items (the per-type AUROC).\\n   - Types: quantifier_scope, cardinality, quantifier_forall_exists, added_condition, dropped_condition, and/or (use the dataset's exact strings), plus implication_direction_or_only.\\n   - Report n, detection and Wilson or bootstrap CIs for TVJT*, B1, A3 and LC.\\n   - Head-to-head on {quantifier_scope ∪ cardinality}: TVJT* − A3, where A3 is predicted near 0.5.\\n   - Supplement with the screen blindspot_supplement: SCOPE_SWAP n=37 and CARD, pairwise detection on gold worlds under the frozen protocol.\\n   - Error-type IDENTIFICATION: map TVJT error_type operator families to L3 types (NEG→negation, QUANT→forall_exists, SCOPE_SWAP→scope, CARD→cardinality, DROP_CONJ→dropped, ADD_CONJ→added, IMPL_REV→implication, AND_OR→and_or, ARG_SWAP→swapped_args, MERGE→conflation). Report the confusion matrix and macro-F1; this is secondary.\\nD. STACKING on P (and on T with y_hard as secondary):\\n   - Cross-fitted logistic regression (GroupKFold by sentence_id, 5 folds, sample weights w, standardized features, C=1.0, fixed).\\n   - Feature sets:\\n     - S0 = [B1, parse_ok]\\n     - S1 = S0 + TVJT*\\n     - S2 = S1 + LC + A3\\n     - S0z = S0 + LC + A3 (the zero-LLM stack without TVJT)\\n   - Report the OOF AUROC_w and the paired-bootstrap ΔAUROC for S1−S0, S2−S0z (TVJT's marginal value over the full gold-free stack) and S2−S0.\\n   - Placebo: S0 + a shuffled TVJT column, whose Δ CI must contain 0.\\nE. CONTAMINATION (Ccon): per metric, AUROC on the originals vs the paraphrases (paired bootstrap over the 104 pairs), the mean score shift orig→para per label class, and |Δ|.\\n   The prediction is TVJT Δ≈0 and B1 possibly negative. Report with and without rename_incomplete rows.\\nF. SYSTEM LEVEL: Kendall τ between each system's mean metric score and its accuracy.\\n   - Accuracy on P is w-weighted; the y_soft accuracy uses all heldout_confirm rows.\\n   - Bootstrap CI over sentences. Note that with 9 systems |τ| ≥ 0.61 is needed for p < .05.\\nG. COST CURVE: $/item (fresh-call cost amortised over cache reuse, and also the no-cache cost) and median seconds per item.\\n   Plot AUROC_w on P against $/item for B1, B1x3, TVJT_lite, TVJT* with 1 vote (C1-equivalent from vote 0) and TVJT* with 3 votes.\\nH. COVERAGE: bridge failures, no-world rate, malformed-JSON rate and canon_fallback rate, per system and corpus. Verbaliser failure rate counted as in iter 1.\\nI. Figures (aii-data-fig-gen):\\n   (1) the AUROC by tercile / n_conditions bin line plot for TVJT*, B1, B1x3, LC and A3, with CIs;\\n   (2) the ΔAUROC(TVJT*−B1) forest plot by bin;\\n   (3) the paired-FA per rewrite kind, iter-1 vs frozen;\\n   (4) the cost-accuracy scatter;\\n   (5) the real-error-type detection bars.\\n\\nDECISION TABLE (written into prereg.json and applied verbatim in results/verdict.json):\\n- CROSSOVER_CONFIRMED: H1 lower bound > 0 AND H2 Δ_top − Δ_bottom lower bound > 0.\\n- CROSSOVER_PARTIAL: H1 holds, H2 is not significant.\\n- NOT_REPLICATED: the H1 CI includes 0.\\n- REVERSED: the H1 upper bound < 0.\\n- Separately:\\n  - REPAIR_OK if the frozen screen G2 passed and the held-out G2 replication (if run) has paired FA ≤ 0.10;\\n  - JUDGE_MATCHED if TVJT* − B1x3 in the top tercile has a lower bound > 0 (otherwise the advantage may be vote averaging);\\n  - STACK_INCREMENT if the S2 − S0z lower bound > 0.\\n\\n=== 8. OUTPUTS ===\\n- method.py: the orchestrator, run as --stage setup|canon_diag|repair|freeze|score_heldout|labels|analysis|export [--limit N].\\n- src/metrics_api2.py exposes the reusable functions:\\n  - tvjt_score(text, fol, config='frozen') -> {score, covered, error_type, worlds, agrees, usd}\\n  - canon(fol_str) -> str\\n  - distinguishing_worlds(fol_str) -> list\\n  Docstrings state precisely what each one measures.\\n- method_out.json in the exp_gen_sol_out schema, validated with aii-json. It holds a dataset per group: screen_real, screen_rewrites, heldout_panel, heldout_top, contamination. Each example has input, output (label), predict_TVJT_frozen, predict_TVJT_iter1, predict_B1, predict_B1x3, predict_TVJT_lite, predict_LC, predict_A3 and predict_B3sc (as strings), plus metadata_* fields.\\n  If it exceeds the limit, split it with aii-file-size-limit, and create the mini and preview variants.\\n- results/: frozen_config.json, prereg.json, freeze_receipt.json, analysis.json, verdict.json, heldout_scores.jsonl, screen_repair_scores.jsonl, cost_ledger.jsonl.\\n- README.md and .aii/manifest.yaml:\\n  - keep: cache/llm*, data/worlds_canon.jsonl and results/. These are paid, irreproducible API outputs.\\n  - delete: .venv (regenerable with the uv commands), nltk_data (redownloadable) and __pycache__.\",", "+  \"implementation_pseudocode\": \"=== 0. SETUP (≈20 min) ===\\nWorkspace: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_qY2a2IS-WLIs/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/<this artifact>/\\n- Read the aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks, aii-openrouter-llms and aii-json skills first.\\n- Create the environment:\\n  uv venv .venv --python 3.12\\n  uv pip install z3-solver nltk spacy numpy pandas scipy scikit-learn statsmodels aiohttp loguru tenacity pyyaml pytest\\n  Then install the en_core_web_sm-3.8.0 wheel and WordNet into ./nltk_data (commands in the Arm B README 'Restoring removed files').\\n- Copy the Arm B files into third_party/armB/: src/*.py, data/screen_set.json, data/worlds_cache.jsonl, data/blindspot_supplement.jsonl, results/screen_scores.jsonl, results/prompts_frozen.json, results/b1_request_example.json, results/analysis.json, and cache/llm/ → cache/llm_iter1/.\\n- Copy Arm A (method.py, src/) into third_party/armA/, and the dataset's src/fol_parse.py into third_party/ds/.\\n- Write src/llm2.py, a wrapper around Arm B's llm.py. It adds:\\n  (i) a GLOBAL cost ledger results/cost_ledger.jsonl that records model, stage, tokens, usd and the cumulative total after EVERY call;\\n  (ii) a HARD breaker: raise BudgetExceeded when cumulative ≥ $9.50, and SOFT stops per stage (table below);\\n  (iii) the cache key sha1(model|prompt_version|messages|temperature|vote_idx);\\n  (iv) concurrency 16 with a semaphore, and 429 backoff (tenacity, exp, max 6).\\n  Use reasoning disabled for gemini-2.5-flash: extra_body {'reasoning': {'enabled': false}}. Match exactly how iter-1 llm.py sent it, and check it in the copied code.\\n- Sanity: re-send 5 iter-1 TVJT prompts through the cache; they must hit the cache at $0.\\n\\nStage budgets (soft stops):\\n- P1 repair on the screen: $1.6\\n- P1b test-retest: $0.15\\n- P2a panel items, all LLM metrics: $2.6\\n- P2c contamination: $0.9\\n- P2b top-tercile expansion: $3.0\\n- flash-lite variant: $0.4\\n- held-out G2 replication: $0.2\\n- reserve: $0.65\\nOrder of execution = P1 → freeze → P2a → P2c → P2b → the rest, so the most valuable held-out evidence is bought first.\\n\\n=== 1. SYNTAX BRIDGE (held-out candidates are not all FOLIO-unicode) ===\\ndef to_folio(fol_str):\\n    ast, err = fc.try_parse(fol_str)          # Arm B parser first\\n    if ast: return ast, 'direct'\\n    a2 = ds_fol_parse.parse(fol_str)           # dataset tolerant parser (ASCII ^ read as AND, LaTeX, snake_case)\\n    s2 = ds_fol_parse.to_unicode_string(a2)    # use its printer; check the function name in fol_parse.py\\n    ast, err = fc.try_parse(s2)\\n    return (ast, 'bridged') if ast else (None, 'unparseable:' + err)\\nLog the bridge coverage per system and corpus. Coverage loss is COUNTED: an uncovered item gets score 0.5 and stays in every AUROC (the iter-1 convention). Every AUROC is also reported on the covered-by-all-metrics subset.\\n\\n=== 2. R1: CANONICAL FORM + canonical mutants/worlds (src/canon.py) ===\\ndef canon(F):\\n  1. Eliminate ↔ and ⊕ only when they sit under a negation; otherwise keep them, to limit blow-up.\\n  2. NNF: push ¬ to atoms. ¬∀ becomes ∃¬ and ¬∃ becomes ∀¬; De Morgan is applied; ¬(A→B) becomes A∧¬B.\\n  3. Restricted-implication normalisation: every implication A→B, and every disjunction directly under ∀ that contains negative literals, is rewritten as (conjunction of the POSITIVE forms of the negative disjuncts) → (disjunction of the remaining disjuncts).\\n     - So A→B, ¬B→¬A and ¬A∨B all map to one form. This kills the CONTRAPOSITIVE and DEMORGAN false alarms.\\n     - If every disjunct is negative, the consequent is the ¬literal that comes last in skeleton order.\\n  4. Prenex with mutants.prenex. Keep the original if prenexing fails (NotPrenexable), and set the flag.\\n  5. Flatten ∧ and ∨ and sort their operands by the NAME-BLIND skeleton string: predicates replaced by 'P/arity', constants by 'c', variables alpha-renamed. Ties are broken by the full string.\\n  6. Alpha-rename bound variables to x, y, z, w, … in order of first occurrence.\\n  7. Size guard: if the canonical tree has more than 3× the original node count, use the original with canon_fallback=True.\\n  8. Verify the canonical form: bounded_equiv(canon(F), F, nmax=4) must be 'equiv'. Otherwise fall back to the original and set the flag.\\n  canon_seed = sha1(skeleton string of canon(F)). This is name-blind, so a RENAME rewrite gets the same seed.\\n\\ndef canonical_worlds(F):\\n  Fc = canon(F)\\n  For op in OPERATORS (CARD with variants 'two' and 'unique', as iter-1 card_variants=True):\\n      m = make_mutant(Fc, op, canon_seed)          # canonical seed, never item_id\\n  Deduplicate the kept mutants by pairwise bounded_equiv; keep the first in OPERATORS order.\\n  For each distinct mutant: w = find_world(Fc, m, prefer=0 fixed, nmax=4), with z3.set_param('smt.random_seed', 0) and 'sat.random_seed' 0.\\n  Deduplicate worlds by isomorphism: canonical key = sorted true_atoms after relabeling elements in order of first appearance. Take the lexicographic minimum over the ≤4! element permutations.\\n  Cap at 12 worlds. Record per world: op, F_value, world_key, and n.\\n  Order the worlds canonically by world_key.\\nCACHE: data/worlds_canon.jsonl, keyed by sha1(to_str(Fc)). Identical canonical forms across systems and rewrites reuse the worlds.\\n\\nZERO-COST DIAGNOSTIC, run BEFORE any LLM call: for each of the 499 screen rewrites, check whether it has the same world multiset as its source gold, up to predicate renaming for RENAME.\\n- Report the 'world-set identity rate' per rewrite kind.\\n- Expected: REORDER, DEMORGAN and CONTRAPOSITIVE at 90% or more. If a kind is below 70%, debug canon() before spending any money.\\n\\n=== 3. R2+R3: JUDGE PROTOCOL (src/judge.py) ===\\nPrompt 'tvjt_v2_conf' is iter-1 TVJT_PROMPT with the SAME HEADER and verbaliser. The answer instruction becomes:\\n  'Answer with JSON only, one entry per situation: {\\\"1\\\": [v, c], ...} where v is 1 if the sentence is TRUE in that situation and 0 if FALSE, and c is your confidence 0-100.'\\nThis compact format keeps output tokens low.\\n\\nWorld-level cache:\\n- key_w = sha1(model | prompt_version | sentence | world_text | vote_idx).\\n- For a target, gather its worlds W in canonical order and drop those already cached for vote k.\\n- Send the uncached ones as ONE batched call, with situations presented in order perm_k(U).\\n  - perm_0 = canonical order, perm_1 = reversed, perm_2 = rotated by ceil(|U|/2).\\n  - The situation labels 1..|U| follow the presented order. Answers are mapped back to worlds and stored per (world, k).\\n- A malformed answer gets one retry with a 'JSON only' reminder. If it still fails, those worlds are uncovered for that vote.\\n\\nScore definitions per target:\\n  p_k(w) = c/100 if v==1 else 1 − c/100          # judged P(sentence TRUE in w)\\n  agree_bin_k(w) = 1[v_k==F_value(w)]\\n  C1 = mean_w agree_bin_0(w)                      # canonical worlds, 1 vote\\n  C2 = mean_w 1[majority_k v_k == F_value(w)]     # 3-vote majority\\n  C3 = mean_w (p̄(w) if F_value(w) else 1 − p̄(w)),  p̄ = mean_k p_k(w)   # continuous, confidence-weighted (PRE-SELECTED default)\\n  error_type = the operator family with the lowest mean agreement (ties broken by OPERATORS order), or 'none' if all agreements are ≥ 0.5.\\nOne call serves C1, C2 and C3, so the grid costs 3 calls per uncached world-set and no more.\\n\\n=== 4. PHASE 1: SCREEN REPAIR (labels allowed = screen only) ===\\nTargets:\\n- 833 real screen candidates;\\n- the 300 golds;\\n- the 499 rewrites.\\nWith the world cache, expect about 1.6k targets and about 900 distinct world-sets.\\n\\nConfigurations:\\n- C0 = iter-1 TVJT, from the copied screen_scores and cache ($0).\\n- C0' = the iter-1 prompt on canonical worlds, vote 0. Run only on the 300 golds and 499 rewrites (≈$0.15). This isolates the R1 effect from the prompt change.\\n- C1, C2, C3 as defined in section 3.\\nScale-up: 50 targets → 300 → all. At each step, check cost and time per target and extrapolate against the stage budget.\\n\\nMetrics per configuration:\\n- AUROC_real under L_bij, which is the iter-1 label and gives a like-for-like comparison with .701. Use a 2000× sentence-clustered bootstrap.\\n- Secondary: AUROC under the screen_gold_audit panel-audited label, joined on sentence_id and system.\\n- Per tercile.\\n- Paired G2 FA: the share of rewrites r with score(r) < score(gold_of_r) − 0.2. Report it overall, for non-RENAME kinds, and per kind.\\n- The iter-1 absolute G2 at τ* (τ* chosen by Youden on the screen) and at 0.5.\\n- Per-operator pairwise detection on gold worlds.\\n- Test-retest (P1b): 150 random screen targets are re-called in a separate cache namespace 'retest'.\\n  - Report per-world verdict agreement with Cohen κ.\\n  - Report the score ICC(2,1) between the two runs for C1 and for C3.\\n  - Report how many worlds have 3-vote disagreement.\\n\\nFREEZE RULE (pre-declared here; do not change after seeing results):\\n- A configuration is ELIGIBLE if (a) its paired G2 FA over ALL rewrites is ≤ 0.10, and (b) its screen AUROC_real(L_bij) is ≥ 0.701 − 0.02 = 0.681.\\n- Pick the eligible configuration with the highest AUROC_real. If two are within 0.01, pick the one with fewer calls: C1 < C2 = C3, then prefer C3 for threshold-freeness.\\n- If none is eligible only because of RENAME (non-RENAME FA ≤ 0.10), pick by the same AUROC rule and record 'G2_all_failed_by_RENAME'. The RENAME FA is then reported as the residual verbaliser/synonym limitation.\\n- If none is eligible at all, go to FALLBACK F1/F2, still on the screen only. Freeze the best configuration anyway, with frozen_G2_pass=false. The crossover question is tested regardless.\\n- Choose the binary threshold τ_frozen by Youden on the screen real items. It is used only for the binary G2 and detection rates.\\n\\nWrite results/frozen_config.json:\\n- config id, prompt text and version, model, T=0, the vote scheme, the scoring formula, τ_frozen and the canon() options;\\n- git hash or sha1 of src/*.py;\\n- the screen metrics that justified it.\\nWrite results/prereg.json with every PHASE-2 hypothesis, test, bin edge and decision rule listed in section 7. Hash both files into results/freeze_receipt.json, with a UTC timestamp. Only then may the held-out LABEL loader run: src/labels.py asserts that freeze_receipt.json exists and that its hashes match.\\nThe held-out SCORING code reads ONLY the input, metadata_item_id, metadata_sentence_id, metadata_system, metadata_corpus and complexity fields. It never reads output or metadata_L3_* fields.\\n\\n=== 5. PHASE 2: HELD-OUT SCORING (label-blind) ===\\nLoad full_data_out_*.json. Sort the parts numerically, following the dataset README loader.\\n\\nItem sets:\\n- P = heldout_confirm rows with metadata_label_source=='panel3' (609).\\n- T = heldout_confirm rows in the top tercile. Check the encoding of metadata_complexity_tercile (0/1/2 or labels) from label_report.json; the top tercile is the highest-composite one. Expect about 316 sentences × 9 = 2,844 rows, all systems, including unparseable ones.\\n- Ccon = contamination rows for systems {gpt-4.1-mini, deepseek-v3.1, llama-3.1-8b}, each paired with its original heldout_confirm row via metadata_original_item_id. That gives 312 pairs, or 624 targets. Extend to all 9 systems if budget remains after P2b.\\n- S_all = all 6,300 heldout_confirm rows. These are needed ONLY for the zero-LLM LC fit and for B3sc.\\n\\nMetrics on P ∪ T ∪ Ccon, identical items throughout:\\n- TVJT* (frozen), with all 3 votes.\\n- TVJT_iter1: the unrepaired iter-1 code (seed=item_id, iter-1 prompt, 1 vote), on P and T only. This is the within-artifact ablation.\\n- B1: the EXACT iter-1 fixed prompt from results/b1_request_example.json and prompts_frozen.json, on gemini-2.5-flash at T=0 with reasoning off, 1 call, output a 0-100 probability.\\n- B1x3: the same prompt with 3 calls. Vote_idx 1 and 2 are sent with the identical prompt and a cache bypass; take the mean of the 3. Run it on P and on T if budget allows. This is the COMPUTE-MATCHED judge.\\n- TVJT_lite: the frozen configuration with google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite as judge, on P only.\\n- parse_ok, both as metadata_parse_ok and as fol_core-bridge coverage.\\n- LC_onecoin (zero-LLM):\\n  - For every sentence in S_all, compute pairwise fc.find_bijections(a_i, a_j)['label'] among its 9 parseable candidates. That is up to 36 pairs × 700 sentences, run on a ProcessPool with 4 workers, spawn, wall 10 s per pair; a timeout counts as not-equivalent and is logged.\\n  - Then run latent_class.run_latent_class (the one-coin EM with an all-wrong state) over all 700 sentences, giving a posterior per candidate.\\n- A3: third_party/armA method.signature_faithfulness(sentence, bridged_fol_str, variant='A3'), on P ∪ T.\\n- B3sc (zero-LLM, standard self-consistency baseline):\\n  - For gpt-4.1-mini and llama-3.1-8b greedy rows, take the share of that system's 5 heldout_samples for the same sentence that are find_bijections-equivalent to the greedy candidate.\\n  - Available on 2/9 systems only; report it on that subset.\\nPersist everything to results/heldout_scores.jsonl, one row per (item_id, metric), with score, covered, error_type_pred, n_worlds, usd, seconds, canon_fallback and bridge.\\n\\nOPTIONAL, if budget remains (P2d): held-out G2 replication, which needs no labels.\\n- Take 100 held-out audited golds (metadata_gold_fol_audited not null, sentence-stratified by corpus).\\n- For each, run mutants.make_rewrites(k=2), which gives about 200 rewrites. Score the golds and the rewrites with TVJT*.\\n- Report the paired FA per kind. Expected cost is about $0.2.\\n\\n=== 6. LABELS (src/labels.py; runs only after freeze_receipt) ===\\n- y_panel (P): metadata_L3_majority mapped to 1 = faithful, with weight w = metadata_L3_sampling_weight.\\n- y_hard (T and other rows): output in {faithful, unfaithful}. unknown is excluded from y_hard metrics and COUNTED.\\n- y_soft:\\n  - panel rows: y_panel;\\n  - otherwise p_faithful = metadata_L3_stratum_p_faithful when present;\\n  - else 0.90 for L1-equivalent rows and 0.44 for L1-non-equivalent rows, from label_report.json (9.9% of equivalent rows are unfaithful; 43.9% of non-equivalent rows are faithful).\\n- Contamination: the output label transferred to the renamed row. Rows with metadata_contamination_rename_incomplete are flagged and reported with and without them.\\n\\n=== 7. ANALYSES (src/analysis2.py; every number goes to results/analysis.json) ===\\nA. Weighted AUROC on P for every metric:\\n   AUC_w = Σ_{i∈pos, j∈neg} w_i w_j [1(s_i>s_j) + .5·1(s_i=s_j)] / (Σw_pos · Σw_neg)\\n   - CI: 2000× cluster bootstrap that resamples sentence_ids within corpus strata (seed 0), reusing the SAME resamples for all metrics so the deltas are paired.\\n   - Also report the unweighted AUROC and the within-sentence pairwise accuracy (a faithful candidate outscores an unfaithful candidate of the same sentence, ties 0.5), over sentences with mixed panel or L1-equivalent labels.\\n   - Also report every AUROC excluding gemini-2.5-flash-generated candidates, because the judge model is also a generator.\\nB. PRE-REGISTERED CROSSOVER (the primary question). Bins: complexity tercile (bottom/mid/top) and n_conditions bins {0-1, 2-3, 4+}.\\n   - H1 (primary): on P restricted to the top tercile, ΔAUROC_w(TVJT* − B1) > 0, with the paired-bootstrap 95% CI lower bound > 0.\\n     Power switch, decided BEFORE labels are read, from label-free counts: if fewer than 150 panel items fall in the top tercile (a label-free count, since L3 selection is known before labels), H1 is evaluated on T with y_soft (soft AUROC, below), and P-top becomes secondary.\\n   - H2 (growth): Δ_top − Δ_bottom > 0 (paired bootstrap CI), and the Δ per n_conditions bin is monotone non-decreasing (reported with CIs).\\n   - H2b (logistic interaction):\\n     - Per metric m, fit a weighted GLM (statsmodels, Binomial, freq_weights = w, cluster-robust SE by sentence_id): y ~ z(s_m) + c + z(s_m):c, with c = complexity_composite (and, separately, n_conditions).\\n     - Report the interaction slope β3 with its CI.\\n     - Joint model: y ~ z(s_TVJT)·c + z(s_B1)·c. Test β(TVJT:c) − β(B1:c) > 0 with a bootstrap CI.\\n   - Soft-label AUROC on T: each item contributes as a positive with weight p and as a negative with weight 1−p (probabilistic ROC).\\n     - Also run multiple imputation: 200 Bernoulli(p) label draws, AUROC per draw, report the mean and the 2.5–97.5% interval, pooled with the cluster bootstrap.\\n     - Also report the hard-label (y_hard) AUROC on T, flagged as biased toward gold vocabulary.\\n   - Repeat B for LC_onecoin, A3, B1x3, TVJT_iter1 and TVJT_lite, to locate which component carries the long-sentence regime.\\n     Key contrasts: TVJT* − B1x3 (compute-matched) and TVJT* − TVJT_iter1 (did the repair cost signal?).\\nC. REAL ERROR TYPES (P, unfaithful items grouped by metadata_L3_primary_error).\\n   - Detection for type e = P(score of the erroneous item < score of a faithful candidate for the SAME sentence). Faithful comparators are panel-faithful or L1-equivalent candidates. Ties count 0.5.\\n   - Fallback when a sentence has no faithful comparator: compare against all panel-faithful items (the per-type AUROC).\\n   - Types: quantifier_scope, cardinality, quantifier_forall_exists, added_condition, dropped_condition, and/or (use the dataset's exact strings), plus implication_direction_or_only.\\n   - Report n, detection and Wilson or bootstrap CIs for TVJT*, B1, A3 and LC.\\n   - Head-to-head on {quantifier_scope ∪ cardinality}: TVJT* − A3, where A3 is predicted near 0.5.\\n   - Supplement with the screen blindspot_supplement: SCOPE_SWAP n=37 and CARD, pairwise detection on gold worlds under the frozen protocol.\\n   - Error-type IDENTIFICATION: map TVJT error_type operator families to L3 types (NEG→negation, QUANT→forall_exists, SCOPE_SWAP→scope, CARD→cardinality, DROP_CONJ→dropped, ADD_CONJ→added, IMPL_REV→implication, AND_OR→and_or, ARG_SWAP→swapped_args, MERGE→conflation). ### [57] ASSISTANT · 2026-09-23 13:58:14 UTC

```
I've written the plan for the TVJT deep test to `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json` in the workspace. I only checked the JSON by eye (quote and escape counts), because I couldn't run a parser here.

TVJT is the metric where the solver builds small worlds and gemini-2.5-flash judges only whether the sentence is true in each one. The plan continues the iter-1 version rather than starting over, reusing its code, screen data and paid LLM cache from the two iter-1 screen experiments.

**Phase 1: fix the false alarms, using the screen only.**
- **Root cause.** Iter-1 picked mutants with a random seed tied to each item's ID. Two equivalent rewrites of the same formula therefore got different mutants, different worlds and different judgments.
- **Fix.** Each formula is put in a canonical form: negations pushed inward, implications and their contrapositives mapped to one shape, conjuncts sorted, variables renamed. The seed comes from that form's structure with predicate names ignored. Equivalent rewrites, including renamed ones, then get the same mutants and worlds.
- **Free check first.** Before any money is spent, the executor measures how often each rewrite kind gets the same worlds as its source. The target is at least 90% for reorder, De Morgan and contrapositive.
- **Judge noise.** Answers are cached per world. Each world gets 3 votes, one per fixed ordering of the worlds in the prompt, and the judge gives true/false plus a 0–100 confidence. A single call yields all three candidate configurations.
- **Freeze rule.** A configuration qualifies if at most 10% of rewrites score more than 0.2 below their source formula, and its screen AUROC is no lower than 0.681. Two backup fixes are listed in case none qualifies.
- **Freeze.** The chosen configuration and the full pre-registration are hashed into a receipt. The held-out label loader refuses to run without it, and the scoring code never reads labels.

**Phase 2: held-out confirmation.**
- **Items.** The 609 panel-labelled items (primary), all ~2.8k top-tercile candidates with hard and soft labels (for power), and the 104 paraphrase pairs for the contamination check.
- **Comparisons.** The same items are scored by:
  - the exact iter-1 judge;
  - that judge with 3 votes, added so TVJT's 3 votes can't explain its advantage;
  - the unrepaired iter-1 TVJT;
  - a cheaper flash-lite TVJT;
  - the latent-class score across the 9 systems;
  - the A3 signature;
  - sampling self-consistency.
- **Main test.** In the top tercile, TVJT beats the judge with a 95% CI lower bound above 0. A second test asks whether the gap grows across terciles and condition-count bins.
- **Other analyses.** Detection of real error types, including scope and cardinality against A3; stacking increments; the contamination shift; system-level ranking; and a cost-versus-accuracy curve. Every AUROC is also reported without gemini-2.5-flash's own candidates, because that model is both the judge and one of the 9 generators.

**Budget.** Expected spend is about $8 of the $10, with a hard stop at $9.50. Work runs in priority order: screen repair, panel items, contamination, then the top-tercile expansion, which is cut down by sentence if money runs short. The plan uses the `cpu_plus` profile.

**Known weaknesses the plan states up front:**
- Labels come from an LLM panel, not humans.
- Round-trip and Monty baselines are left to the parallel iter-2 experiment and joined by item ID if its scores exist.
- "Heavily conditioned" means many connectives, not legal exceptions, because public gold has almost none.
- Real scope and cardinality errors are only about 7% of errors, so those estimates will have wide intervals.
```
